The migration touches three kinds of code: visual snapshots of a shadcn form, where a single select without `ui:placeholder` goes from blank to "Select ..." and a multiple select with one shows it in place of "Select ..."; a `translateString` table of your own, which gains the `TranslatableString.SelectPlaceholder` key to translate; and anything building a custom widget on the deep-imported `FancySelect`/`FancyMultiSelect`, where `FancySelect`'s `placeholder` prop is now required and `FancyMultiSelect`'s `ariaPlaceholder` prop is removed.

### `AltDateWidget` date parts are named in every theme BREAKING CHANGE

Each select an `AltDateWidget` (and `AltDateTimeWidget`) renders is a control of its own: `DateElement` gives it [`dateElementId()`](../api-reference/utility-functions.md#dateelementid) as both its id and its `name`, and the `name` prop it used to take is ignored and deprecated.
Each part is named by the field's label and the part, such as `When, year`, in every theme, `@rjsf/mantine` included, even when the field hides its label, so the parts of two date fields stay distinct; only a field with no label at all leaves the part alone, such as `year` ([#5355](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5355)).
Each part is also described by the field's description, help and errors, which no part was linked to before.
A test querying a part by its own name, or by the value it displays, no longer matches when the field has a label:

```diff
-screen.getByRole('combobox', { name: 'year' });
+screen.getByRole('combobox', { name: 'When, year' });
```

The parts' names, placeholders and `@rjsf/daisyui`'s visible part labels go through `translateString` as `TranslatableString.YearLabel`, `MonthLabel`, `DayLabel`, `HourLabel`, `MinuteLabel` and `SecondLabel`, and the way the field's label and the part are combined goes through `TranslatableString.DateElementAriaLabel` (`'%1, %2'`), so a locale can reorder them or change the separator ([#5357](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5357)).
Their english defaults are the lowercase part names the widgets displayed before, so a form without a custom `translateString` displays the same text.

`DateElement` hands the name and the description to the registry's `SelectWidget` as its `aria-label` and `aria-describedby`, which every theme's `SelectWidget` now gives to the control a screen reader focuses through the new [`widgetAriaProps()`](../api-reference/utility-functions.md#widgetariaprops), describing it by the caller's ids, followed by the id of the selected option's description, the one element it renders itself, while it shows one.
A replacement `SelectWidget` must do the same, or the date parts it renders are left unnamed and undescribed; no other widget is required to honor the two props:

```tsx
import { widgetAriaProps } from '@rjsf/utils';

function MySelectWidget(props: WidgetProps) {
  return <select id={props.id} {...widgetAriaProps(props)}>{/* ... */}</select>;
}
```

Four theme changes follow from this.
`@rjsf/mui`'s `SelectWidget` describes its combobox rather than the field's root, and an `aria-label` or `aria-describedby` set in an `htmlInput` given through `rjsfSlotProps` still wins over both.
`@rjsf/mantine`'s `AltDateWidget` no longer renders a hidden copy of a title the field doesn't show, which nothing refers to now that each part carries the title in its own `aria-label`.
`@rjsf/fluentui-rc`'s `SelectWidget` no longer renders an empty `<label>` for a field with no label, which named the dropdown by nothing.
`@rjsf/shadcn`'s single `SelectWidget` trigger is a `combobox`, which holds the option it displays as its value, so a date part named by its `aria-label` still announces its selected option.

### `AltDateWidget` hands an unset date part on as `undefined`, and `useAltDateWidgetProps()` returns `buttonsDisabled` BREAKING CHANGE

`getDateElementProps()`, and so `useAltDateWidgetProps()`'s `elements`, give a part nobody has picked yet an `undefined` `value`, where the `DateObject` behind them holds `-1`, and `DateElement` hands the `SelectWidget` `undefined` for a `-1` it is given ([#5378](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5378)).
Every theme's parts now report it to `onFocus` and `onBlur` as `undefined`, as `@rjsf/core`'s, `@rjsf/react-bootstrap`'s and `@rjsf/mantine`'s already did, where `@rjsf/chakra-ui`'s reported `''` and the other themes' `-1`.
A replacement `SelectWidget`, a custom `AltDateWidget` that renders its own controls from `elements`, or an `onBlur` handler, that tells an unset part by `''` or `-1` should tell it by `undefined`.

`useAltDateWidgetProps()` returns `buttonsDisabled`, true when the widget is disabled or read-only, a required member of `UseAltDateWidgetResult`, so a hand-built one, such as a mocked return of the hook, has to include it ([#5378](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5378)).

### `enumOptionValueEncoder()` is removed in favor of `enumOptionsDomValues()` BREAKING CHANGE

`enumOptionValueEncoder()` encoded one option at a time, but in the `realValue` format an option's DOM value now depends on the rest of the list: options whose `String()` is the same, such as `1` and `'1'` or `true` and `'true'`, used to share a DOM value, so the second of them could not be picked and picking it wrote the first one's value ([#5315](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5315)).
The new [`enumOptionsDomValues()`](#new-non-validator-utility-functions) encodes the whole list in one pass, giving each of those options its prefixed index (`__rjsf_index:1`) instead, and `enumOptionValueDecoder()` and `enumOptionSelectedValue()` encode against the list the same way.
It also encodes an object, array, `null` or empty string option, and a string starting with the prefix, as its index prefixed with the new `ENUM_OPTION_INDEX_PREFIX` constant, where v6 encoded objects and arrays as the bare index and `null` as the empty string, so code that parses these DOM values itself needs to expect the prefix ([#5309](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5309)).

A custom widget encodes its options once per render and reads each option's value at its position:

```tsx
// v6
value={enumOptionValueEncoder(option.value, index, optionValueFormat)}
// v7
const domValues = enumOptionsDomValues(enumOptions, optionValueFormat);
// ...
value={domValues[index]}
```

For options from `groupEnumOptions()`, read `domValues[option.index]`, since `index` is the option's position in the ungrouped list.

### `@rjsf/utils` schema functions take a `SchemaContext` in place of the `validator` BREAKING CHANGE

The validator-based schema functions in `@rjsf/utils` used to take the `customMergeAllOf` and `defaultFormStateBehavior` of a form as separate parameters.
Each of those functions had to copy them, one by one, into every schema function it called, and several calls left one out, so a `customMergeAllOf` silently stopped applying partway down a call chain ([#4383](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/4383), [#4385](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4385)).

Those settings now travel together with the `validator` in a `SchemaContext`:

```ts
interface SchemaContext<S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema, F extends FormContextType = FormContextType> {
  readonly validator: ValidatorType<S, F>;
  readonly customMergeAllOf?: CustomMergeAllOf<S>;
  readonly defaultFormStateBehavior?: DefaultFormStateBehavior;
}
```

Every function that took a `validator` takes a `SchemaContext` in its place, and passes it along whole to every schema function it calls, so dropping the `customMergeAllOf` would also mean dropping the `validator`.
The separate `customMergeAllOf` and `defaultFormStateBehavior` parameters are gone, and the parameters that followed them move up a position; every other parameter, `rootSchema` included, is unchanged:

```diff
-const resolvedSchema = retrieveSchema(validator, schema, rootSchema, formData, customMergeAllOf);
-const isSelectField = isSelect(validator, schema, rootSchema, customMergeAllOf);
+const context: SchemaContext = { validator, customMergeAllOf, defaultFormStateBehavior };
+const resolvedSchema = retrieveSchema(context, schema, rootSchema, formData);
+const isSelectField = isSelect(context, schema, rootSchema);
```

| Function                      | v6 parameters                                                                                       | v7 parameters                                                                                                  |
| ----------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `findFieldInSchema()`         | `validator, rootSchema, schema, path, formData?, customMergeAllOf?`                                 | `context, rootSchema, schema, path, formData?`                                                                 |
| `findSelectedOptionInXxxOf()` | `validator, rootSchema, schema, fallbackField, xxx, formData?, customMergeAllOf?`                   | `context, rootSchema, schema, fallbackField, xxx, formData?`                                                   |
| `getClosestMatchingOption()`  | `validator, rootSchema, formData, options, selectedOption?, discriminatorField?, customMergeAllOf?` | `context, rootSchema, formData, options, selectedOption?, discriminatorField?`                                 |
| `getDisplayLabel()`           | `validator, schema, uiSchema?, rootSchema?, globalOptions?, customMergeAllOf?`                      | `context, schema, uiSchema?, rootSchema?, globalOptions?`                                                      |
| `getFirstMatchingOption()`    | `validator, formData, options, rootSchema, discriminatorField?`                                     | `context, formData, options, rootSchema, discriminatorField?`                                                  |
| `getFromSchema()`             | `validator, rootSchema, schema, path, defaultValue, customMergeAllOf?`                              | `context, rootSchema, schema, path, defaultValue`                                                              |
| `getUiRequiredErrorSchema()`  | _new in v7_                                                                                         | `context, rootSchema, uiSchema?, formData?, uiSchemaDefinitions?, globalUiOptions?, formContext?, fieldState?` |
| `isFilesArray()`              | `validator, schema, uiSchema?, rootSchema?, customMergeAllOf?`                                      | `context, schema, uiSchema?, rootSchema?`                                                                      |
| `isMultiSelect()`             | `validator, schema, rootSchema?, customMergeAllOf?`                                                 | `context, schema, rootSchema?`                                                                                 |
| `isSelect()`                  | `validator, theSchema, rootSchema?, customMergeAllOf?`                                              | `context, theSchema, rootSchema?`                                                                              |
| `omitExtraData()`             | `validator, schema, rootSchema?, formData?, customMergeAllOf?`                                      | `context, schema, rootSchema?, formData?, fieldState?`                                                         |
| `retrieveSchema()`            | `validator, schema, rootSchema?, rawFormData?, customMergeAllOf?, resolveAnyOfOrOneOfRefs?`         | `context, schema, rootSchema?, rawFormData?, resolveAnyOfOrOneOfRefs?, selectedOption?`                        |
| `sanitizeDataForNewSchema()`  | `validator, rootSchema, newSchema?, oldSchema?, data?, customMergeAllOf?`                           | `context, rootSchema, newSchema?, oldSchema?, data?`                                                           |

`retrieveSchema()` is the one to double check: a v6 call passing `resolveAnyOfOrOneOfRefs` passed `customMergeAllOf` (or `undefined`) before it, so drop that argument rather than only replacing the `validator`.

`getDefaultFormState()` is the exception: it had the longest list of optional parameters, most of them easy to pass in the wrong order, so it now takes a `SchemaContext` followed by a `GetDefaultFormStateProps` object:

```diff
-getDefaultFormState(validator, schema, formData, rootSchema, false, defaultFormStateBehavior, customMergeAllOf, undefined, uiSchema);
+getDefaultFormState({ validator, customMergeAllOf, defaultFormStateBehavior }, { schema, formData, rootSchema, uiSchema });
```

Its props are `schema`, `formData?`, `rootSchema?`, `includeUndefinedValues?`, `initialDefaultsGenerated?`, `uiSchema?` and `uiSchemaDefinitions?`, with the same meanings and defaults the positional parameters had.

`createSchemaUtils()` and `SchemaUtilsType.doesSchemaUtilsDiffer()` take a `SchemaContext` and the `rootSchema`:

```diff
-const schemaUtils = createSchemaUtils(validator, rootSchema, defaultFormStateBehavior, customMergeAllOf);
+const schemaUtils = createSchemaUtils({ validator, defaultFormStateBehavior, customMergeAllOf }, rootSchema);
```

`SchemaUtilsType`'s own methods supply the context and root schema themselves, so they are unchanged.

`ValidatorType` keeps its v6 signatures: `validateFormData()`, `isValid()` and `rawValidation()` take no `SchemaContext`.
A custom validator still has to change for this release, since `isValid()` is now handed `{ $ref }` pointers into the `rootSchema` it is passed; see [Validators are handed pointers into the root schema](#validators-are-handed-pointers-into-the-root-schema-breaking-change).
The context carries a `validator`, so handing one to a validator would have meant every implementation discarding the validator it was given in favor of itself; the two things a validator actually needed the settings for are supplied another way instead.

`validateFormData()` takes one new optional trailing parameter, `getCustomValidateFormData`: a function returning the form data to hand `customValidate`, with the defaults already applied.
`Form` supplies it from its own `SchemaUtils`, so those defaults honor the form's `customMergeAllOf` and `defaultFormStateBehavior`, which they never did before.
Left out, the validator computes them itself exactly as it did in v6, so a direct caller keeps working:

```diff
 validator.validateFormData(formData, schema, customValidate, transformErrors, uiSchema);
+validator.validateFormData(formData, schema, customValidate, transformErrors, uiSchema, () => schemaUtils.getDefaultFormState(schema, formData, true, undefined, uiSchema));
```

It is a function rather than the data itself for two reasons: defaults that legitimately come out `undefined` are then an answer the validator can tell apart from "nothing was supplied", and an implementation that computes the defaults its own way never makes the caller compute them as well.

A **precompiled** validator takes the form's `customMergeAllOf` at construction rather than per call, since it is tied to one root schema compiled once:

```diff
-const validator = createPrecompiledValidator(validateFns, schema);
+const validator = createPrecompiledValidator(validateFns, schema, { customMergeAllOf });
```

It is used when the validator resolves its own root schema to check the schema it is handed, which is what previously failed with "The schema associated with the precompiled validator differs from the rootSchema provided for validation" for a form with a `customMergeAllOf` and an `allOf` in its root schema.
`compileSchemaValidators()` and `compileSchemaValidatorsCode()` take no `customMergeAllOf`: they compile the subschemas the root names, and a schema a merge makes is not compiled (see [Validators are handed pointers into the root schema](#validators-are-handed-pointers-into-the-root-schema-breaking-change)).

The `computeSkipPopulate()` callback of [`defaultFormStateBehavior.arrayMinItems`](../api-reference/form-props.md#arrayminitemscomputeskippopulate) receives the `SchemaContext` in effect in place of the `validator`, so any schema function it calls can be handed the form's `customMergeAllOf` too:

```diff
-computeSkipPopulate: (validator, schema, rootSchema) => !isSelect(validator, schema.items, rootSchema),
+computeSkipPopulate: (context, schema, rootSchema) => !isSelect(context, schema.items, rootSchema),
```

The helpers that are exported only through deep imports of `@rjsf/utils/lib/schema/*` follow the same rule.
`computeDefaults()`, `getObjectDefaults()`, `getArrayDefaults()`, `getDefaultBasedOnSchemaType()`, `ensureFormDataMatchingSchema()` and `calculateIndexScore()` take a `SchemaContext` in place of the `validator` and no longer take a `customMergeAllOf` or `defaultFormStateBehavior`, including as members of the props object `computeDefaults()` and its siblings take.
The `retrieveSchema()` helpers such as `retrieveSchemaInternal()`, `resolveSchema()` and `resolveDependencies()` are no longer exported: see [validators are handed pointers into the root schema](#validators-are-handed-pointers-into-the-root-schema-breaking-change).
`computeDefaults()` and its siblings no longer take a `_recurseList` in their props: each call starts the recursion afresh at the schema it is given.

Four places that used to drop a setting now apply it:

- `SchemaUtilsType.omitExtraData()` never passed the form's `customMergeAllOf` on, so the `allOf`s it merged were always merged the default way.
- An `allOf` reached through a `$ref` option of a `oneOf` inside `dependencies` was always merged the default way.
- The defaults handed to `customValidate` were computed with neither the `customMergeAllOf` nor the `defaultFormStateBehavior` of the form.
- The precompiled validators of `@rjsf/validator-ajv8` and `@rjsf/validator-ata` check the schema they are handed against their root schema, resolving that root schema when it isn't an exact match. They resolved it with the default `allOf` merge, so under `liveValidate` a form with a `customMergeAllOf` and an `allOf` in its root schema failed with "The schema associated with the precompiled validator differs from the rootSchema provided for validation".
  They now resolve it with the `customMergeAllOf` they were constructed with, so the error no longer comes from `validateFormData()`, nor from a schema function scoring `oneOf`/`anyOf` options or resolving a condition against that root schema.

A form that sets either of them sees it applied in those places from now on.

With a **precompiled** validator, give the form's `customMergeAllOf` to `createPrecompiledValidator()`, as above, so the root-schema check resolves with the merge the form resolves with.

### `canExpand()` and `allowsAdditionalProperties()` take a `SchemaContext` BREAKING CHANGE

Whether an object naming no `additionalProperties` takes extra keys is now a form setting, [`additionalPropertiesWhenAbsent`](#additionalpropertieswhenabsent-reads-an-object-naming-no-additionalproperties-as-the-spec-does), which travels in the `SchemaContext`.
So `canExpand()` and `allowsAdditionalProperties()` take the `SchemaContext` as their first parameter, as the other schema utilities do, rather than answering for an absent keyword on their own ([#2137](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/2137)).
`SchemaUtilsType` gains `canExpand(schema, uiSchema, formData)` and `allowsAdditionalProperties(schema)`, which pass the form's own `SchemaContext`, and every theme's `ObjectFieldTemplate` now calls `registry.schemaUtils.canExpand()`.

A custom `ObjectFieldTemplate` does the same:

```tsx
// v6
{canExpand(schema, uiSchema, formData) && <AddButton onClick={onAddProperty} />}
// v7
{registry.schemaUtils.canExpand(schema, uiSchema, formData) && <AddButton onClick={onAddProperty} />}
```

Code that calls either function outside a form passes a `SchemaContext` of its own, such as `canExpand({ validator }, schema, uiSchema, formData)`; neither reads the `validator`, only `additionalPropertiesWhenAbsent`, so a `SchemaContext` without that setting answers as v6 did.
A `SchemaUtilsType` implementation of your own needs the two new methods.

### An `$id` under an unknown keyword, such as `components` or `x-*`, names no schema BREAKING CHANGE

Embedded schemas are found by `$id` through the schema's structure: only the keywords that hold a schema, an array of schemas or a map of names to schemas are walked.
Under a container that is none of these, such as an OpenAPI-style `components` block or an `x-*` vendor key, an `$id` is data. It is no longer found as an embedded schema, and it no longer changes what the `$ref`s beside and below it resolve against, also where a `$ref` reaches the schema holding it. A schema such a `$ref` reaches is still read as a schema, and its own `$ref`s resolve against the resource holding the container, so the usual OpenAPI layout, with `components` at the root and `#/components/...` refs, keeps working.
Only one case breaks: a `$ref` to an `$id` declared inside the container, which now throws `Could not find a definition for ...`.
If a schema relies on it, move those schemas under `$defs` (draft 2020-12) or `definitions` (draft 7) so the walk reaches them.
A `$ref` beside such an `$id` resolves where it threw: with `$defs: { real: { $id: 'n.json', type: 'boolean' }, s: { type: 'string' } }`, `components: { Pet: { type: 'object', properties: { n: { $id: 'n.json', $ref: '#/$defs/s' } } } }` and `properties: { pet: { $ref: '#/components/Pet' } }` under a root whose `$id` is `https://e.com/root.json`, `root_pet_n` is a string field, where the form threw `Could not find a definition for #/$defs/s.`.

One symptom is worth knowing: AJV collects `$id`s by walking every key, so it still finds an `$id` kept under `components` or `x-*`.
A form whose schema relies on that passes validation but throws `Could not find a definition for ...` when it renders.

### `localToUTC()` can return `null` BREAKING CHANGE

`localToUTC()` takes `unknown` and returns `string | null | undefined`, where its inferred return type was `string | undefined`.
`null` isn't a new result: `localToUTC()` has always returned it for a value `Date` can't read, since `toJSON()` gives `null` for an invalid date though its type says `string`.
A value `Date` throws for, such as a bigint or a symbol, now gives `null` as well instead of throwing.
Code that assigns the result to `string | undefined` no longer compiles; map `null` to the value it should store:

```ts
// Before
const utc: string | undefined = localToUTC(value);

// After
const utc: string | undefined = localToUTC(value) ?? undefined;
```

### A non-object field's `anyOf`/`oneOf` option renders with its parent's keywords BREAKING CHANGE

A field with `anyOf`/`oneOf` options is rendered entirely by its selected option, unless it is an object, whose own `ObjectField` renders its properties beside the option.
The option used to inherit only the field's `required` list and `type`, so anything else the field declared never reached the control that renders it ([#5359](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5359)).
An array whose options only limit its length rendered "Unsupported field schema ... Missing items definition." in place of its items, and a `format: 'date'` string rendered a plain text input.

```json
{
  "type": "array",
  "items": { "type": "string", "enum": ["a", "b"] },
  "uniqueItems": true,
  "oneOf": [{ "minItems": 1 }, { "maxItems": 1 }]
}
```

The option of a field that is not an object now inherits every keyword of the field that describes its value, so this schema renders a multi-select.
That includes keywords that change how a field that already rendered looks, such as `format`, `enum`, `examples` and `readOnly`, so a `format: 'date'` string now renders a date input.
Switching to another option checks the value against that option as it renders, inherited keywords included, so an array whose options only limit its length keeps its items, where checking it against the bare option, which is no array, cleared them.
A value that is neither an array nor an object, such as a string, is still cleared by every switch, even between two options that both describe it.
A tuple keeps its items the same way when both options describe it alike, and an array the selected option has a `const` for is replaced with that `const` when it doesn't equal it, as one the option selects whole arrays from with an `enum` is cleared when the `enum` doesn't list it, unless the option switched away from declared the same `const` or `enum`, which already rejected the array.
Where both options render the items alike, with the same uiSchema and a field per item, and the switch leaves the array's length as it was, an item field's own errors stay with the item, as its field stays in place. An option that is a select over whole arrays renders one control for the array, and one holding options of its own renders its items anew, so a switch to or from either drops them.
The field's `title`, `description`, `deprecated` and `default` stay with the field rather than being repeated on the option.
The field's own template labels it and draws its description whichever option is selected, an array or a checkbox included, and the option's field draws its own title and description only where they say something else, as [a field's label, description, help and errors are drawn where its plan says](#a-fields-label-description-help-and-errors-are-drawn-where-its-plan-says-breaking-change) describes.
A bound both the field and the option declare, such as `maxItems`, `maxLength` or `minimum`, keeps the stricter of the two, since a value has to meet both: an option's `maxItems: 5` beside the field's `maxItems: 3` stops Add at three items, and switching to that option trims the array to three.
An option's `true` subschema, such as `items: true`, adds nothing to meet, so it leaves the field's subschema in place rather than rendering every item as an unsupported field, a tuple included.
An option's `items` schema beside the field's tuple applies to each of its positions, and to the items past it where the tuple declares `additionalItems`; a tuple declaring none stays at its length, as `ArrayField` offers it no Add, and so does an option's tuple beside the field's `items` schema.
A field's `false` subschema, which nothing meets, stays in place of the option's in the same way.
An `enum` the option declares is its own list as it stands, since its `ui:enumNames` are read by position against it: a value the field's `enum` doesn't list stays, and validation rejects it. Two `required` lists ask for every name either does.
A `not`, or a conditional (`if`, `then` and `else` together), that the option declares replaces the field's as a whole, rather than being merged with it into one neither declared.
Any other keyword both declare, such as a `format` or a `pattern`, is the option's.
A subschema either declares through a `$ref`, such as `items: { "$ref": "#/$defs/short" }`, is left to the field's `allOf` merge, which meets the two once the `$ref` is resolved: met before it, the keywords beside the `$ref` would be layered over the ones it names, and a looser bound would win.
`ui:required` is checked against the option as it renders, too: a `ui:required` field within the field's `items` is checked where an option's `items: true` leaves them in place, and one the field's `false` subschema forbids is not.
The form's defaults are computed from the option as it renders as well, so an option's `minItems: 1` beside the field's `minItems: 2` starts the form with two items, each holding the defaults of the field's `items`, whether the option is written in place or as a `$ref`, and a tuple's options add to the defaults of its positions.
An option written as a `$ref` is followed only to the schema it names, so an `allOf` within it is still left to the `allOf` defaults behavior. One that inherits nothing from the field, such as the option of a typeless field, is computed as before, taking the field's `default` only when there is no data; one that inherits something takes the field's `default` as an option written in place does, beneath its data and its own `default`.
A field that is not an object and declares both `anyOf` and `oneOf` renders only the selected option of the keyword `SchemaField` reads, the `anyOf` unless it is empty, so its defaults are that option's alone, where they used to include those of the other keyword's selected option too. An object field still adds both.
The bounds are read as draft-06 and later define them, where `exclusiveMinimum` and `exclusiveMaximum` are numbers of their own.
The draft-04 form, a boolean that makes `minimum` or `maximum` exclusive, is not supported: beside a numeric bound from the other schema it can make a bound exclusive that neither schema did, as with a field's `maximum: 3` and an option's `{ maximum: 5, exclusiveMaximum: true }`, which renders as less than 3.
A schema written for draft-04 or OpenAPI 3.0 should use the numeric form, `exclusiveMaximum: 5` in place of `maximum: 5, exclusiveMaximum: true`.

An object that names no `type` of its own, but has `properties`, now passes `object` on to options that name no type either, as a `type: 'object'` field already did.
Its options used to render "Unsupported field schema ... Unknown field type undefined."; they now render as the typed field's do.

### A field's label, description, help and errors are drawn where its plan says BREAKING CHANGE

`SchemaField` now plans each field before rendering it ([#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490)): from the field and widget it renders, and the [`presentation`](../api-reference/form-props.md#presentation) the registry merges from `@rjsf/core`, the theme and the `Form`, it decides who draws the field's label, description, help and errors, under which ids, and the attributes its control carries.
The plan reaches the `FieldTemplate`, the field and its widget as a `presentation` prop, as described in [`resolveFieldPresentation()`](../api-reference/utility-functions.md#resolvefieldpresentation), and the props templates and widgets already read are now derived from it.
`displayLabel`, `rawErrors`, `hideLabel` and `getDisplayLabel()` are marked `@deprecated` and kept through v7 for a template or widget rendered without a plan; read the plan instead, or [`templateDrawsLabel()`](../api-reference/utility-functions.md#templatedrawslabel), [`hasTemplateErrors()`](../api-reference/utility-functions.md#hastemplateerrors), [`widgetDrawsLabel()`](../api-reference/utility-functions.md#widgetdrawslabel) and [`hasWidgetErrors()`](../api-reference/utility-functions.md#haswidgeterrors), which fall back to them only without one.
The `rawErrors` a field and an `ArrayFieldTemplate` or `ObjectFieldTemplate` are handed, which carry the errors whatever `hideError` says, and `getVisibleErrors()` and `hasVisibleErrors()`, which apply `hideError` to them, are marked `@deprecated` too, with `VisibleErrorsProps`, their parameter type ([#5325](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5325)): the plan's `presentation.errors.messages` has `ui:hideError` applied, and a component draws them only where `presentation.errors.by` is `'self'`, since the template draws them under `'template'`, which is what the new `getErrorsToDraw()` gives it; for an error state, read `hasWidgetErrors()` in a widget, `hasTemplateErrors()` in a template, or whether `presentation.errors.messages` has any in a field. All five stay through v7, and the props are still handed over in full, for a component that renders the hidden errors its own way.
With a plan, the plan wins: core's and every theme's templates and widgets ignore a `hideLabel`, `displayLabel` or `rawErrors` handed alongside one, so a custom field that renders a widget with its plan and `hideLabel` to hide the widget's label should hand it a plan whose label isn't the widget's instead:

- `displayLabel` is true only where the template draws the label. In a theme whose widgets draw their own label, as `@rjsf/mui`'s, `@rjsf/fluentui-rc`'s, `@rjsf/chakra-ui`'s and `@rjsf/mantine`'s do, it is false for nearly every field, so a custom `FieldTemplate` there should no longer gate the description on it. Read it through [`templateDrawsLabel()`](../api-reference/utility-functions.md#templatedrawslabel), which reads the plan and falls back to `displayLabel` only for a template rendered without one.
- `ArrayFieldItemTemplateProps.displayLabel` says whether a label sits above the item's control, from the plan the item's `SchemaField` builds, where it was `getDisplayLabel()` of the item's schema and its own `uiSchema`: a label its template draws, which is the `displayLabel` the item's own `FieldTemplate` is handed, or one its field or widget draws itself where its declaration places it `labelPlacement: 'above'`, as `@rjsf/fluentui-rc`, `@rjsf/chakra-ui` and `@rjsf/mantine` declare their widgets' labels. So the item's buttons are placed by the label above its control. It is now `false` for an item with no label text, a `null` item, a hidden item, a boolean under `ui:widget: 'checkbox'`, and a custom field drawing its own label whichever of `ui:field`, `ui:options.field` or a `$id` names it, unless its declaration places that label above, and `true` for a custom field declared `{ label: 'template' }`, a boolean whose titled `oneOf` constants render a select, and an object `anyOf`/`oneOf`, whose selector its template labels, as does an item the fallback UI (`useFallbackUiForUnsupportedType`) renders with a choice of types, whose type selector comes first and is labelled for the type whatever the item's title, unless `ui:label` is `false` or the item is hidden, which hides the selector with it. In `@rjsf/mui`, whose text inputs and selects draw their label inside the input's outline, it is `false` for those.
- `description` and `rawDescription`, `help` and `rawHelp`, and `errors` are handed to the template only where it draws them. Render them when they are present rather than deciding again from `displayLabel` or the `uiSchema`.
- `hideLabel` tells a widget that the template, or a field above rendering the same id, draws its label. A widget that keyed its own description on `hideLabel` should take it from [`getWidgetDescription()`](../api-reference/utility-functions.md#getwidgetdescription) instead, and one that draws its own label should ask [`widgetDrawsLabel()`](../api-reference/utility-functions.md#widgetdrawslabel), which falls back to `hideLabel` only for a widget rendered without a plan.
- `required` still says whether the field is required; a template drawing the label should mark it from `presentation.label.requiredMarker`, as `@rjsf/core`'s does, which leaves the `*` off a checkbox, or a widget its theme declares `uncheckedIsFalse`, whose schema doesn't demand `true` ([#5388](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5388)).
- A template drawing the label should give it the `for` that [`templateLabelFor()`](../api-reference/utility-functions.md#templatelabelfor) gives, as `@rjsf/core`'s does, which is none where `presentation.control.attributes['aria-labelledby']` is set: that control, such as the wrapper of a group of radios or checkboxes, is one a `for` can't name, and it is named through the label's `presentation.label.id` instead.

What renders differently:

- **`anyOf`/`oneOf`.** The field's template labels the field and draws its description, help and errors, whichever option is selected, so the option's control no longer draws a second label ([#5358](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5358)) and the errors no longer disappear with a `null` option. An option's own title and description render beneath the field's only where they say something else, the description under [`optionDescriptionId()`](../api-reference/utility-functions.md#optiondescriptionid). An option given no `uiSchema` of its own no longer takes the field's `ui:title` and `ui:description` in place of its own. An option with no title of its own takes the field's label rather than its property name: as its control's label in a theme whose selector draws the field's label itself, as `@rjsf/mui`'s, `@rjsf/chakra-ui`'s, `@rjsf/fluentui-rc`'s and `@rjsf/mantine`'s do, and as its heading while an optional data control keeps the selector off screen, with the field's description beneath that heading. An object with no properties of its own beside its options is labelled as the field rendering those options is; one with properties keeps drawing its heading, under the `XxxOf` ids it renders beside the options with. As in v6, the option selector is neither required nor marked so, even for a required field, since it picks an option index rather than a value and holds one unless its widget offers an empty choice ([#5527](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5527)). No built-in selector does, except `@rjsf/mantine`'s where the field's `ui:options` set `allowDeselect` or `clearable`, and clearing the choice clears the field's data with it. The field's label carries the marker, and so does a heading the selected option draws of its own, as a titled object or array option does. In a theme whose selector draws the field's label itself, only the selected option's own label or heading carries it, so an option that draws none, such as a `null` option or an object with no title of its own, leaves the field unmarked. Also as in v6, a discriminated cell a `LayoutGridField` lays out is required with its cell, since its selector is the only control for the discriminator it writes into the data, where an `anyOf`/`oneOf` field's selected option renders the discriminator as a control of its own.
- **Custom fields.** A field given as `ui:field`, `ui:options.field` or a `$id` draws its own label and description, as one given as `ui:field` already did: one named by `ui:options.field` or chosen by a `$id` used to get the template's label. Declare the parts the template should draw for it under its registry key. A custom field is handed its own plan as `presentation`, so one that spreads its props into a field it renders itself, such as `StringField`, should leave `presentation` out of them, or that field takes the custom field's label and ids for its own. One that renders `SchemaField` under its own `id`, as a wrapper adding markup around the field does, leaves the label and description it was planned to draw to the field it renders, which draws them, while its template draws the help and errors once. A template `label` declared through `fieldDefault` would also reach objects and arrays, which have no control for the template's label to name, so they would draw no heading:

  ```tsx
  <Form presentation={{ fields: { MyField: { label: 'template', description: 'template' } } }} />
  ```

- **`ui:label: false`** hides the description along with the label in every theme, as it already did outside `@rjsf/antd`.
- **An empty `ui:title` or `ui:description`** on an object no longer hides its heading or description: the schema's `title` or `description` shows instead, as it already did for an array and for a field whose template draws its label. Set `ui:label: false` to hide both.
- **`null` fields**, and a field rendered through `UnsupportedFieldTemplate`, draw their own title and description, through `TitleFieldTemplate` and `DescriptionFieldTemplate`, rather than a label naming no control.
- **The fallback UI** labels its value from outside only where the value is a single control; an object, an array or a checkbox value draws its own heading or label, with the field's description beneath it, as before.
- **Labels a widget draws** take the field's label as the template's does: an array item whose `items` schema has a `title` is labelled by it rather than by the numbered title (`Tags-1`), and a deprecated field's carries the `DeprecatedLabel` marker.
- **Headings.** `@rjsf/core`'s `TitleFieldTemplate` puts the [`titleId()`](../api-reference/utility-functions.md#titleid) it is handed on a `<span>` around the title's text rather than on its `<legend>`, since an object's option selector is named by that id and would otherwise take the `*` and the optional data control's button into its name. A selector or style aimed at `legend#root__title` should aim at `legend #root__title`, and a custom `TitleFieldTemplate` should put its `id` on the title's text alone.
- **Group labels.** `@rjsf/core`'s `FieldTemplate` draws the label of a `RadioWidget` or `CheckboxesWidget` with no `for`, which named the group's wrapper `<div>`, an element a `for` can't label; the group is named by the label's [`fieldLabelId()`](../api-reference/utility-functions.md#fieldlabelid) through `aria-labelledby`. A selector aimed at `label[for="root_color"]` for such a field should aim at `#root_color__label`, which is on a `<span>` around the label's text, as a heading's id is, so a group named by it takes no `*`; `label:has(#root_color__label)` matches the label itself. `RatingWidget` renders its stars as a `radiogroup` on the wrapper `<div>`, named the same way, where its label's `for` named its hidden `<input>`; the wrapper carries the field's id in that `<input>`'s place, so a selector aimed at `input#root_rating` should aim at `#root_rating`. `AltDateWidget`, and so `AltDateTimeWidget`, wraps its list of date parts in a `group` `<div>` carrying the field's id, named the same way, around the `<ul>`, which keeps its list role, where its label's `for` named an id no date part carried, so a selector aimed at `label[for="root_when"]` should aim at `#root_when__label`. An `anyOf`/`oneOf` field whose options are all objects, arrays or `null`s has its label drawn with no `for` too, since a container or a `null` renders no control at the field's id for a `for` to name, and an array option counts so whatever widget it renders, its multi-select or file input named by the label through `aria-labelledby`, as the option selector is whatever its field's options, so a selector aimed at `label[for="root_pet"]` for such a field should aim at `#root_pet__label`; one with an option of another type, such as a string, or a `$ref`, keeps the `for`. A widget of your own registered as `RadioWidget`, `CheckboxesWidget`, `RatingWidget`, `AltDateWidget` or `AltDateTimeWidget` takes that declaration with the key, so it is named only through the `aria-labelledby` it spreads from [`getControlAttributes()`](../api-reference/utility-functions.md#getcontrolattributes) or [`getGroupAttributes()`](../api-reference/utility-functions.md#getgroupattributes); one that renders a single control under its `id` and spreads neither should be declared `labelledBy: 'for'` under its key, as `presentation={{ widgets: { CheckboxesWidget: { labelledBy: 'for' } } }}` declares one registered as `CheckboxesWidget`, to keep the label's `for`. One that draws its own label, such as a date picker registered as `AltDateWidget`, should be declared `label: 'self'` under its key, so the template draws no second label. `@rjsf/antd` declares its `RangeWidget` `labelledBy: 'aria-labelledby'`, since a `for` reaches only the slider's root rather than its handle, so a widget of your own registered as `RangeWidget` under antd's theme takes that declaration too. A wrapper of antd's slider registered under a key of your own, or the slider itself registered under one while `RangeWidget` holds another widget, takes no declaration, so declare that key `labelledBy: 'aria-labelledby'` for its handle to be named; one passed to `ui:widget` as a component has no key to declare, so register it under one. Under antd, `#root_color__label` is likewise on a `<span>` around the label's text, which also carries the label's `title`, so `label:has(#root_color__label)` matches the label. antd's slider handle takes no `aria-describedby` or `aria-invalid`, so a range's errors, description and help are announced only through the template ([#5560](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5560)).
- **An untitled field** has no label, so a required array item with no title no longer renders a label holding only the required marker; the input is still `required`.
- **`additionalProperties`.** The key input is labelled wherever the entry's value is, whoever draws that label, so a checkbox entry's key is now labelled too, and where `ui:label: false` hides that label, the key input is named by it through `aria-label`. An object or array entry's heading shows its `ui:title`, or its schema `title`, where it has one, as before.
- **Layout grid `anyOf`/`oneOf` cells.** A discriminated union a `LayoutGridField` lays out draws its label whatever its schema's `type`, as one with no `type` already did: one declaring `type: 'object'` beside its `oneOf` drew none, and left its selector unnamed. Set `ui:label: false` on the cell to hide it; the selector is still named by its text.
- **Accessibility.** A control's `aria-describedby` names only elements that render, an `AltDateWidget`'s parts included, it is `aria-invalid` while it has errors, except a radio, whose `radiogroup` is instead, the template's label carries [`fieldLabelId()`](../api-reference/utility-functions.md#fieldlabelid) on its text, and a group of radios or checkboxes is labelled by that label through `aria-labelledby`. A `SelectWidget` of your own describes an `AltDateWidget`'s parts only by reading its props through [`widgetAriaProps()`](../api-reference/utility-functions.md#widgetariaprops): `DateElement` hands it a plan of its own rather than an `aria-describedby`.
- **`@rjsf/mantine`'s errors and `RatingWidget`.** Errors its widgets don't show, those of a custom widget or `ui:field`, `@rjsf/core`'s `RatingWidget` or a widget whose `inputWrapperOrder` leaves out `'error'`, are shown by `FieldErrorTemplate`, where they were announced from a hidden copy and never shown; see [the Mantine ids section](#rjsfmantine-widgets-reference-rjsfs-ids-for-labels-and-descriptions-breaking-change). A custom `ui:field` that hands one of this theme's widgets `rawErrors` but not its plan, as `FieldProps.presentation` advises for a field that doesn't declare `WIDGET_FIELD_PRESENTATION`, shows the field's errors twice: the widget, given no plan, draws them, and so does the template. Pass the widget `hideError` or no `rawErrors`, or declare `WIDGET_FIELD_PRESENTATION` for the field and hand the widget its `presentation`. The theme declares `@rjsf/core`'s `RatingWidget` `label: 'template'` and `description: 'template'`, since it draws neither, so a widget of your own registered as `RatingWidget` that draws its own label should be declared `label: 'self'` under that key, as the "Group labels" entry above says, and one that draws its own description `description: 'self'`, or it shows them twice, or, where it draws its label only while `widgetDrawsLabel()` is true and spreads no `aria-labelledby` from `getControlAttributes()` or `getGroupAttributes()`, its control is left with no name.
- **`@rjsf/chakra-ui`'s ids, `RatingWidget` and alt-date widgets.** The element a user focuses carries the field's id rather than an id Ark generated: the text input, the textarea, the number input's and the checkbox's `<input>`, the select's trigger and the slider's thumb, and in a radio or checkboxes group, which carries the field's id itself, each `<input>` its [`optionId()`](../api-reference/utility-functions.md#optionid). A selector aimed at an Ark id, such as `select:root_color:trigger` or `checkbox:root_color-0:input`, should aim at `#root_color` or the option's id. `TitleField` puts the title id on a `<span>` around the title's text inside its `<h5>` rather than on the box around the heading, its optional data control and its separator, so a selector or style aimed at `#root__title` now reaches that `<span>`. The select's label has no `for`, so a selector aimed at `label[for="root_color"]` should aim at `#root_color__label`, and a checkboxes group's label is drawn as a `<div>` styled as the fieldset's legend rather than as a `<legend>`, so a `legend` selector no longer reaches it. The theme declares `@rjsf/core`'s `RatingWidget` and its own `AltDateWidget` and `AltDateTimeWidget` `label: 'template'`, since none of them draws a label, so a widget of your own registered under one of those keys that draws its own label should be declared `label: 'self'` under its key, as the "Group labels" entry above says, or it shows two labels, or, where it draws its label only while `widgetDrawsLabel()` is true and spreads no `aria-labelledby` from `getControlAttributes()` or `getGroupAttributes()`, its control is left with no name.
- **`@rjsf/daisyui`'s headings and triggers.** `TitleField` puts the title id on a `<span>` around the title's text inside its `<h2>`, and `ArrayFieldTitleTemplate` on one inside its `<h3>`, each followed by a `*` for a required object or array, so a selector or style aimed at `div#root__title` or `h3#root_tags__title` should aim at `#root__title` or `#root_tags__title`. A radio, checkboxes, rating or alt-date field's label has no `for`, as `@rjsf/core`'s doesn't, so a selector aimed at `label[for="root_color"]` should aim at `label:has(#root_color__label)`. `FieldTemplate`'s label puts its label id on a `<span>` around the label's text, for every field, as `@rjsf/core`'s does, so a selector aimed at `label#root_name__label` should aim at `label #root_name__label`, which matches that `<span>`, or at `label:has(#root_name__label)` for the label together with its `*`. `SelectWidget`'s trigger, like the date pickers', has the `combobox` role, so a query for it by the `button` role should use `combobox`. Both take the keys that role promises: Down Arrow, or Alt+Down Arrow, opens either, a date picker's moving focus onto its selected day, else today, and in `SelectWidget` Down and Up Arrow move into the listbox and between its options, Home and End to its first and last, from the trigger too, and Enter or Space on an option of a single select, or a click on one, chooses it and closes the listbox, as Escape does, returning focus to the trigger. The trigger is `SelectWidget`'s one tab stop, so Tab moves on to the next field rather than through the options, and Shift+Tab from an option closes the listbox as it returns focus to the trigger. The listbox opens on daisyUI's `:focus-within`, so it stays open while focus is anywhere inside it, an option included, and closes once focus leaves it.
- **`@rjsf/fluentui-rc`'s groups, `required` and headings.** `RadioWidget` and `CheckboxesWidget` draw their label with no `for`, which named the radio group's wrapper or, for `CheckboxesWidget`, an id no element had, and name the group by it through `aria-labelledby`, so a selector aimed at `label[for="root_color"]` should aim at `#root_color__label`. `FieldTemplate`'s `Field` no longer takes `required`, which Fluent handed every control inside it, so only the controls the plan marks are `required`, and a custom widget whose Fluent control took `required` from the `Field` sets it itself. `TitleField` puts the title id on a `<span>` inside its `<h5>`, so a selector aimed at `div#root__title` should aim at `#root__title`. `SelectWidget` draws no label holding only a required field's `*`, and a dropdown no label names carries an `aria-label`: the field's label, its placeholder or the translated prompt, so a snapshot of an untitled select gains it. The theme declares `@rjsf/core`'s `RatingWidget`, `AltDateWidget` and `AltDateTimeWidget` `label: 'template'`, since none of them draws a label, so a widget of your own registered under one of those keys that draws its own label should be declared `label: 'self'` under its key, as the "Group labels" entry above says, or it shows two labels, or, where it draws its label only while `widgetDrawsLabel()` is true and spreads no `aria-labelledby` from `getControlAttributes()` or `getGroupAttributes()`, its control is left with no name.
- **`@rjsf/mui`'s slider, groups, headings and errors.** `RangeWidget` puts the field's id on its slider's `<input>` rather than on the slider's root, so a selector aimed at `span#root_x` should aim at `input#root_x`, except for a slider of several values, which keeps the id on its root. A radio or checkboxes field's label has no `for`, which named the group's wrapper, and names the group through `aria-labelledby`, so a selector aimed at `label[for="root_color"]` should aim at `#root_color__label`. `TitleField` puts the title id on a `<span>` around the title's text inside its heading, so a selector or style aimed at `div#root__title` should aim at `#root__title`. `FieldErrorTemplate`'s messages no longer carry ids of their own, so a selector aimed at `#root_x__error-0` should aim at the items of `#root_x__error`. The theme declares `@rjsf/core`'s `RatingWidget`, `AltDateWidget` and `AltDateTimeWidget` `label: 'template'`, since none of them draws a label, so a widget of your own registered under one of those keys that draws its own label, such as a MUI `Rating` registered as `RatingWidget` or a date picker registered as `AltDateWidget`, should be declared `label: 'self'` under its key, as the "Group labels" entry above says, or it shows two labels, or, where it draws its label only while `widgetDrawsLabel()` is true and spreads no `aria-labelledby` from `getControlAttributes()` or `getGroupAttributes()`, its control is left with no name.
- **`@rjsf/react-bootstrap`'s groups, headings, help and descriptions.** `RadioWidget`'s and `CheckboxesWidget`'s `Form.Group` carries the field's id and the `radiogroup` or `group` role, named by the template's label, which `FieldTemplate` draws with no `for`, as it does for a rating or alt-date field, so a selector aimed at `label[for="root_color"]` should aim at `label:has(#root_color__label)`. `FieldTemplate`'s label puts its plan's label id on a `<span>` around the label's text, as core's does, so a selector aimed at `label#root_color__label` should aim at `label #root_color__label`, which matches that `<span>`, or at `label:has(#root_color__label)`, which matches the label with its `*`. `TitleField` puts the title id on a `<span>` around the title's text, followed by a `*` for a required object or array, so a selector aimed at `div#root__title` should aim at `span#root__title`. `TitleField` draws the `title` it is handed rather than `ui:title` over it, and `ArrayFieldTemplate` draws only the heading `ArrayField` hands it as `title`, as core's do, so a custom field rendering either with a `title` of its own sees that title drawn, where the field's `ui:title` replaced it. `FieldHelpTemplate` and `FieldTemplate`'s description render their `Form.Text` as a `<div>`, so a selector or style aimed at `small#root__help` should aim at `div#root__help`.
- **`@rjsf/shadcn`'s range, groups, headings, help and descriptions.** `RangeWidget` puts the field's id on its slider's thumb rather than its root, and the label names the thumb with no `for`. An empty range draws its thumb at its minimum, so a screen reader announces that minimum as its value, though the form holds no value until the thumb is pressed or moved. `RadioWidget`'s and `CheckboxesWidget`'s wrapper carries the field's id and the `radiogroup` or `group` role, named by the template's label, which `FieldTemplate` draws with no `for`, so a selector aimed at `label[for="root_color"]` should aim at `label:has(#root_color__label)`. `FieldTemplate`'s label puts its plan's label id on a `<span>` around the label's text, for every field, as core's does, so a selector aimed at `label#root_name__label` should aim at `label #root_name__label`, which matches that `<span>`, or at `label:has(#root_name__label)`, which matches the label with its `*`. `TitleField` puts the title id on a `<span>` around the title's text, so a selector aimed at `div#root__title` should aim at `span#root__title`. `FieldHelpTemplate` and `FieldTemplate`'s description render in a `<div>` rather than a `<span>`, so a selector or style aimed at `span#root__help` should aim at `div#root__help`.

### Breaking changes: schema resolution

`@rjsf/utils` now parses each root schema once into a graph of its subschemas, and `retrieveSchema()`, `getDefaultFormState()` and the form all stop a recursive `$ref` by one rule.
A `$ref` is compared by the subschema it reaches, so two spellings of one target are one `$ref`.
A field stops, with an Expand Cycle button in the form, where the first `$ref` it enters is already on the way down to it, or where any `$ref` it enters already occurs twice on that way.
Every schema that overflowed the stack or ran out of memory on earlier releases now terminates.
The sections below list what changes, each with the results before and after. "Before" is what 6.11.0, the last release, gives.

#### `retrieveSchema()` resolves one level at a time BREAKING CHANGE

`retrieveSchema()` merges the schema's own `$ref`, `allOf`, `if`/`then`/`else`, `dependencies` and the patterns its properties match, as before.
A `$ref` nested under `properties`, `items` or an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option is no longer resolved in place. It is handed out as written:

```ts
const rootSchema = {
  definitions: { address: { type: 'object', properties: { city: { type: 'string' } } } },
  type: 'object',
  properties: { home: { $ref: '#/definitions/address' } },
};
const retrieved = retrieveSchema(context, rootSchema, rootSchema);
// Before: retrieved.properties.home is the address definition, with its `properties`
// After: retrieved.properties.home is `{ $ref: '#/definitions/address' }`
const home = retrieveSchema(context, retrieved.properties.home, rootSchema);
// home is the address definition, with its `properties`
```

`SchemaField` and the other fields already retrieve each subschema they render, so a form renders the same.
If your code reads a nested subschema out of one `retrieveSchema()` result, retrieve that subschema again, as the example does.

Every subschema `retrieveSchema()` hands out carries the `RJSF_PROVENANCE_KEY` Symbol, which records where it came from. A spread copy keeps it.
Retrieving such a subschema resolves it as the field it is. A recursive `$ref` is therefore flagged with `RJSF_REF_CYCLE_KEY` at the same place however many times a schema is retrieved, and retrieving a schema that `retrieveSchema()` returned gives it back unchanged:

```ts
const once = retrieveSchema(context, rootSchema, rootSchema, formData);
retrieveSchema(context, once, rootSchema, formData);
// Before: one more level of every recursive `$ref` is unrolled, still flagged as a cycle
// After: equal to `once`
```

A schema without that Symbol, such as one built in code, is resolved from the top, as before.

#### Expand Cycle reveals one level per click BREAKING CHANGE

`expandRefCycle()` returns the schema of a flagged field resolved one level further, which `CyclicSchemaField` renders when its Expand Cycle button is clicked (see [Option selection and cycle expansion are form state](#option-selection-and-cycle-expansion-are-form-state)). One click now reveals one more level of the recursion, whatever the recursion goes through:

- `{ type: 'object', properties: { child: { $ref: '#' } } }`, after clicking `#root_child_child-button`. Before: the next button is `#root_child_child_child_child-button`. After: it is `#root_child_child_child-button`.
- `pl = { type: 'object', properties: { a: { type: 'string' }, child: { $ref: pl } } }` with a root `$ref` to `pl`, after clicking `#root_child-button`. Before: the next button is `#root_child_child_child-button`. After: it is `#root_child_child-button`.
- A recursion through a `then` or an `allOf` overflowed the stack or ran out of memory (below). It now reveals one level per click as well.

A few recursions now stop at a different place:

- The first row of an array under a root `$ref` stops a recursive property where every other row does. With `node = { properties: { name, kids: { type: 'array', items: { $ref: node } }, best: { $ref: node } } }`, a root `$ref` to `node` and the data `{ kids: [{}] }`: before, `root_kids_0_best` rendered and `root_kids_0_best_best` was collapsed. After, `root_kids_0_best` is collapsed, as it already was for deeper rows and under a root that is not a `$ref`.
- A `$ref` spelled `#/definitions/%41` is the same target as `#/definitions/A`. With `A = { properties: { name, child: { $ref: '#/definitions/%41' } } }` and a root `$ref` to `A`: before, `root_child_child` was collapsed. After, `root_child` is collapsed, as it is when `child` is written `{ $ref: '#/definitions/A' }`.
- A recursion through a chain of aliases stops at the first alias, since each alias is compared by the definition it reaches. With `node = { type: 'object', properties: { n: { type: 'string' }, c: { $ref: A1 } } }`, `A1 = { $ref: A2 }`, `A2 = { $ref: node }` and a root `$ref` to `node`: before, `root_c_n` rendered and `root_c_c` was collapsed. After, `root_c` is collapsed.
- Each row of an array that the data holds renders the rows the data has below it, under any root. With `node = { properties: { v, kids: { $ref: list } } }`, `list = { type: 'array', items: { $ref: node } }`, a root that is `node` written out rather than a `$ref` to it, and the data `{ v: 'a', kids: [{ v: 'b', kids: [{ v: 'c', kids: [{ v: 'd' }] }] }] }`: before, `root_kids_0_kids` was collapsed, hiding `c` and `d`. After, every row renders, down to `root_kids_0_kids_0_kids_0_v`, as it already did under a root `$ref` to `node`.
- A row added to an array of a recursive definition gets the defaults the form's first row got. With `A = { properties: { v, b: { $ref: B } } }`, `B = { type: 'array', minItems: 1, items: { $ref: A } }` and a root `$ref` to `A`, after clicking `#root_b__add`: before, the new row was filled five levels deep, down to `root_b_1_b_0_b_0_b_0_b_0_v`. After, it is filled as `root_b_0` is, down to `root_b_1_b_0_b_0_v`.

Some forms ran out of memory or overflowed the stack, and they now render with an Expand Cycle button where the recursion comes back round:

- an `allOf` or a `then` around the recursive `$ref`, as in `child: { allOf: [{ $ref: node }] }` ([#5501](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5501)), or a `then` that declares the recursive property. Before: out of memory for the `allOf`, a stack overflow for the `then`. After: `#root_child-button`.
- an `anyOf` option that refers back to the schema offering it, as in `node = { anyOf: [{ $ref: node }, { properties: { x } }] }`. Before: out of memory. After: the selector alone, and `root_x` once the second option is selected.
- definitions that all refer to one another. Four definitions, each with a `$ref` property to the three others: before, out of memory. After, 16 inputs and 33 Expand Cycle buttons. The size of such a form still grows factorially with the number of definitions.

#### Markers on resolved schemas BREAKING CHANGE

- `resolveUiSchema()` resolves a field's `$ref` as `retrieveSchema()` does, an `$anchor` included, so a field written `{ $ref: '#nd' }` gets the `ui:definitions` entries of its definition's `oneOf`/`anyOf` options. Before, it logged `could not resolve $ref in resolveUiSchema` with `Could not find a definition for ...` and left them out.
- A schema that merges itself back in no longer leaves a literal `$ref` on the result. For `Pet` with `then: { $ref: Cat }` and `Cat = { allOf: [{ $ref: Pet }, ...] }`: before, the merged `Pet` had a `$ref` key. After, it has none.
- `RJSF_REF_KEY` and `RJSF_REF_CYCLE_KEY` are still added, at the places described above.
- An extra key of the data whose `additionalProperties` or `patternProperties` `$ref` names a definition with an `allOf` keeps `RJSF_REF_KEY`, so it gets that definition's `ui:definitions` entry, as a property does. With `A = { allOf: [{ type: 'object', properties: { a: { type: 'string' } } }] }`, `additionalProperties: { $ref: A }`, the data `{ k: {} }` and `'ui:definitions': { '#/definitions/A': { 'ui:description': 'FROM_A' } }`: before, the `allOf` merge dropped the marker and `k` showed no `FROM_A`. After, it does, as `properties: { k: { $ref: A } }` already did.
- With the default `@rjsf/validator-ajv8`, whose Ajv is draft-07, a `$ref` into a 2019-09 or 2020-12 root resolves from the first validation on. Before, the first `isValid()` call with such a root returned `false` and only later calls with the same root validated, so the first `getDefaultFormState()` call could take the wrong branch. With `$schema` 2020-12, `$defs: { I: { properties: { k: { const: 'x' } } } }`, `properties: { k: { type: 'string', default: 'x' } }`, `if: { $ref: '#/$defs/I' }`, `then: { properties: { t: { type: 'string', default: 't' } } }`, `else: { properties: { e: { type: 'string', default: 'e' } } }` and the data `{ k: 'x' }`, on a fresh validator: before, the defaults were `{ k: 'x', e: 'e' }`. After, they are `{ k: 'x', t: 't' }`. The form rendered `root_k` and `root_t` before and after. To validate such a root under its own dialect, pass `AjvClass: Ajv2020` (or `Ajv2019`) to `customizeValidator()`.
- Under a draft 2019-09 or 2020-12 root, its `$schema` written with or without a trailing `#`, a `$ref` to an `$anchor` resolves in `retrieveSchema()`, `getDefaultFormState()` and the form, as in `resolveUiSchema()`. Under draft-07 and earlier `$anchor` is no keyword, so such a `$ref` still throws. With a 2020-12 root whose `$id` is `https://e.x/root`, `$defs: { n: { $anchor: 'nodeA', type: 'object', properties: { v: { type: 'string', default: 'v' } } } }` and `properties: { p: { $ref: '#nodeA' } }`: before, the form and `getDefaultFormState()` threw `Could not find a definition for https://e.x/root#nodeA.`. After, the form renders `root_p_v`, and the defaults are `{ p: { v: 'v' } }`.

#### Keywords beside a `$ref` BREAKING CHANGE

A `$ref` with keywords beside it is read as JSON Schema reads it, under every draft, in the form, the defaults, option scoring and `omitExtraData()`: as the `allOf` of those keywords and of the schema the `$ref` names.
Before, the keywords beside a field's own `$ref` overrode the schema it names keyword by keyword under every draft, and under draft-07 and earlier a `$ref` reached through another `$ref` dropped the keywords beside it.
With `Pet = { type: 'object', properties: { name: { type: 'string', default: 'n' } }, required: ['name'] }` and `cat: { $ref: Pet, properties: { meow: { type: 'string', default: 'm' } } }`:

- the form renders `root_cat_meow` beside `root_cat_name`, where it rendered `root_cat_meow` alone, and `Pet`'s `required` applies to `cat`;
- the defaults are `{ cat: { meow: 'm', name: 'n' } }`, where they were `{ cat: { meow: 'm' } }`. The keywords beside a `$ref` are no `allOf` the schema declares, so they are filled whatever the `allOf` defaults behavior;
- `omitExtraData()`, which the `omitExtraData` and `liveOmit` props run, keeps `{ cat: { name: 'x', meow: 'y' } }` of the data `{ cat: { name: 'x', meow: 'y', z: 1 } }`, where it kept `{ cat: { meow: 'y' } }`.

The same holds where another `$ref` reaches such a `$ref`, as `v: { $ref: M }` reaches `M = { $ref: O, properties: { b: { type: 'string', default: 'b' } } }` with `O = { properties: { a: { type: 'string', default: 'a' } } }`.
Under draft-07 the form renders `root_v_b` beside `root_v_a`, where it rendered `root_v_a` alone, and the defaults are `{ v: { b: 'b', a: 'a' } }`, where they were `{ v: { a: 'a' } }`.
With the options `[{ type: 'object', properties: { a: { type: 'string' } } }, { $ref: M }]` and no selected option, `getClosestMatchingOption()` picks the second option for `{ b: 'y' }`, where with `@rjsf/validator-ajv8` and `@rjsf/validator-cfworker` it matched no option and returned the selected option, `-1` (`@rjsf/validator-ata` already picked the second); it picks the first for `{ a: 'y' }`, as before.
The keywords beside the `$ref` come first in the merge, so a field renders them first, and where both sides declare an annotation, theirs wins.
A `customMergeAllOf` merges the two as it merges an `allOf`, in the form, the defaults, option scoring and `omitExtraData()` alike.

Annotations are read as before: a `$ref` whose only other keywords are `title`, `description`, `default`, `examples`, `readOnly`, `writeOnly`, `deprecated`, `$comment`, `$id`, `$anchor`, `$defs`, `definitions` and `$schema` reads as the schema it names, with the annotations beside it over its own.
So `{ $ref: Status, default: 'active' }` defaults to `'active'` whatever `Status` declares, and a `$ref` beside nothing but `$defs` fills its target's defaults: with `$defs: { a: { $defs: { n: { type: 'number', default: 7 } }, $ref: '#/$defs/a/$defs/n' } }` and `properties: { l: { $ref: '#/$defs/a' } }`, the defaults are `{ l: 7 }` where they were `{}`.

A `dependencies` entry is qualified by the properties of the schema that declares it, as before, so one in the schema a `$ref` names is not qualified by the `properties` beside the `$ref`, and one beside a `$ref` with no `properties` beside it is qualified by any key the data holds.

#### `getDefaultFormState()` folds over the schema graph BREAKING CHANGE

`getDefaultFormState()` reads the same schema graph as `retrieveSchema()`.
Each field's schema is merged from its `$ref`, its `allOf` (under `allOf: 'populateDefaults'`, or where the field has an `if`), its `then`/`else` and its `dependencies`, as the form renders the field.
A recursive `$ref` stops where the form shows an Expand Cycle button. Its defaults are filled one level further, once, as if that button had been clicked.
The `dependencies` are applied again until the defaults bring no new key that qualifies another one.

It terminates on every recursive schema, including the ones that overflowed the stack: the shapes of [#5500](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5500), [#5452](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5452) a and c, and definitions whose `dependencies` refer to one another.
Five definitions that all refer to one another took about 18 s per call, and now take a fraction of a second.

These overflowed the stack, and now give:

- **A recursion behind an `allOf` under `allOf: 'populateDefaults'` is filled one level further, as every other recursion is.** With `outer = { allOf: [{ $ref: inner }, { properties: { extra: { default: 'e' } } }] }`, `inner = { properties: { name: { default: 'n' }, back: { $ref: outer } } }` and `properties: { a: { $ref: outer } }`: `{ a: { name: 'n', extra: 'e', back: { name: 'n', extra: 'e' } } }`. With `tags = { type: 'array', minItems: 1, items: { $ref: tags }, allOf: [{ minItems: 1 }] }`, `required: ['t']` and `properties: { t: { $ref: tags } }`: `{ t: [[undefined]] }`.
- **A schema at the root that refers back to itself through a `then` fills the property it recurses through**, as under a property. With `Pet = { properties: { petType: { default: 'cat' }, friend: { $ref: Pet } }, if: { properties: { petType: { const: 'cat' } } }, then: { $ref: Cat } }`, `Cat = { allOf: [{ $ref: Pet }, { properties: { hunts: { default: 'h' } } }] }` and a root `$ref` to `Pet`: `{ petType: 'cat', hunts: 'h', friend: { petType: 'cat', hunts: 'h' } }`.
- **A property that a `patternProperties` pattern matches and that refers back to its definition is filled one level further**, as a property without the pattern is, and keeps the pattern's defaults where it stops. With `node = { type: 'object', properties: { child: { $ref: node } }, patternProperties: { '^c': { type: 'object', properties: { tag: { oneOf: [{ const: 'p' }] } } } } }` and a root `$ref` to `node`: `{ child: { tag: 'p', child: { tag: 'p' } } }`.
- **Chains through several cycles of definitions stop at a fixed depth.** With cycles of two and three definitions, each holding a property `k: { allOf: [{ $ref: Leaf }, { $ref: next }] }`, under one root `allOf` and `allOf: 'populateDefaults'`, the defaults stop after a few levels rather than following the least common multiple of the cycle lengths.

These defaults change:

- **An array whose `minItems` sits in an `allOf` is filled to the depth the same array with `minItems` beside its `items` is.** With `pl = { properties: { a: { default: 'x' }, child: { $ref: pl } } }` and `arr: { type: 'array', items: { $ref: pl }, allOf: [{ minItems: 1 }] }` under `allOf: 'populateDefaults'`: before `{ arr: [{ a: 'x', child: { a: 'x', child: { a: 'x' } } }] }`, after `{ arr: [{ a: 'x', child: { a: 'x' } }] }`.
- **Extra keys of the data are filled down to where the schema stops recursing.** With `map = { type: 'object', properties: { name: { default: 'm' } }, additionalProperties: { $ref: map } }`, a root `$ref` to `map` and the data `{ k: { j: { i: {} } } }`: before `{ name: 'm', k: { name: 'm', j: { name: 'm', i: { name: 'm' } } } }`, after `{ name: 'm', k: { name: 'm', j: { i: {} } } }`. The data below is kept as it is, and the form still renders every level. Keys a `patternProperties` pattern matches stop the same way: with `node = { type: 'object', properties: { n: { default: 'n' } }, patternProperties: { '^c': { $ref: node } } }`, a root `$ref` to `node` and the data `{ c1: { c2: { c3: { c4: {} } } } }`: before `{ n: 'n', c1: { n: 'n', c2: { n: 'n', c3: { n: 'n', c4: { n: 'n' } } } } }`, after `{ n: 'n', c1: { n: 'n', c2: { c3: { c4: {} } } } }`.
- **The keys a `default` object names no longer grow on every call.** With the same `map` and `default: { k: {} }`: the first call gives `{ name: 'm', k: { name: 'm' } }`, as before. Before, each further call on that result added one more level. After, every call gives the same result.
- **A `then` gives a property without a `type` the default its branch declares**, as the form renders it. With `p: { if: {}, then: { $ref: S, default: 'sib' } }` and `S = { type: 'string', default: 'def' }`: before `{}`, after `{ p: 'sib' }`. With `p: { if: {}, then: { $ref: O, required: ['a'] } }` and `O = { type: 'object', properties: { a, b }, default: { a: 'da', b: 'db' } }`: before `{}`, after `{ p: { a: 'da', b: 'db' } }`.
- **A chain of `dependencies` is filled in one call.** With `k0` having a default and each `k(i-1)` a dependency adding `k(i)` with a default, nine keys long: before, the first call gave `{ k0, k1 }` and each further call added one key. After, the first call gives `k0` to `k8`.
- **The keywords beside a `$ref` apply at every level, as they do in the form.** With `node = { properties: { name: { default: 'n' }, child: { $ref: node, properties: { own: { default: 'o' } } } } }` and a root `$ref` to `node`: before `{ name: 'n', child: { name: 'n', child: { own: 'o' } } }`, after `{ name: 'n', child: { own: 'o', name: 'n', child: { own: 'o' } } }`.
- **A `default` beside a `$ref` overrides the referenced schema's wherever it stands**, as it already did on a property, an array's `items` or the root: `{ properties: { status: { $ref: Status, default: 'active' } } }` with `Status = { type: 'string', default: 'draft' }` gives `{ status: 'active' }` before and after. With `s = { type: 'string', default: 'target' }`, `node = { properties: { v: { $ref: s, default: 'beside' }, child: { $ref: node } } }` and a root `$ref` to `node`: before `{ v: 'beside', child: { v: 'target' } }`, after `{ v: 'beside', child: { v: 'beside' } }`. Along a chain of aliases, with `alias = { $ref: s, default: 'alias' }` and `inner: { $ref: alias }`: before `{ inner: 'target' }`, after `{ inner: 'alias' }`. `computeDefaults()` on `{ $ref: s, default: 'beside' }`: before `'target'`, after `'beside'`. In a draft-07 tuple position, `arr: { type: 'array', items: [{ $ref: s, default: 'beside' }, { $ref: s }] }`: before `{ arr: ['target', 'target'] }`, after `{ arr: ['beside', 'target'] }`, and the form's first input shows `beside`. In `additionalItems`, `arr: { type: 'array', minItems: 2, items: [{ type: 'string' }], additionalItems: { $ref: s, default: 'beside' } }`: before `{ arr: [undefined, 'target'] }`, after `{ arr: [undefined, 'beside'] }`.
- **An `anyOf`/`oneOf` option written as a `$ref` is merged with its field's `default` as an option written in place is.** With `O = { type: 'object', properties: { a: { type: 'number' }, b: { type: 'number' } } }`, `o: { default: { a: 1 }, anyOf: [{ $ref: O }] }` and the data `{ o: { b: 2 } }`: before `{ o: { b: 2 } }`, after `{ o: { a: 1, b: 2 } }`, which the option written in place already gave. With `S = { type: 'string', default: 'child' }` and `s: { default: 'parent', anyOf: [{ $ref: S }] }` under `nestedDefaultsPrecedence: 'ancestorWins'`: before `{ s: 'parent' }`, after `{ s: 'child' }`, as for `S` written in place. An option `{ $ref: s, default: 'beside' }` gives `'beside'` where it gave `'target'`.
- **An `allOf` behind the `$ref` of an extra key of the data follows the `allOf` defaults behavior**, as one behind a property's `$ref` does. With `A = { allOf: [{ type: 'object', properties: { a: { type: 'string', default: 'a' } } }] }`, `{ type: 'object', additionalProperties: { $ref: A } }` and the data `{ k: {} }`, under the default `allOf: 'skipDefaults'`: before `{ k: { a: 'a' } }`, and `a` showed in the form, after `{ k: {} }`. A key a `patternProperties` pattern matches changes the same way. The keywords beside a `$ref` are no such `allOf`: under 2019-09 and 2020-12, with `M = { $ref: O, properties: { b: { default: 'b' } } }` used as `additionalProperties: { $ref: M }`, the defaults are `{ k: { b: 'b', a: 'a' } }` before and after. Before, only an `additionalProperties` of the root schema was filled: under a property, `p: { type: 'object', additionalProperties: { $ref: A } }` with `{ p: { k: {} } }` gave `{ p: { k: {} } }` before and after. Set `allOf: 'populateDefaults'` to fill it: `{ k: { a: 'a' } }` before and after.
- **Under `allOf: 'populateDefaults'`, a `dependencies` entry inside an `allOf` is filled when a default qualifies it**, as one beside `properties` is. With `p: { allOf: [{ dependencies: { a: { properties: { c: { type: 'string', default: 'c' } } } } }, { type: 'object', properties: { a: { type: 'string', default: 'x' } } }] }` and no data: before `{ p: { a: 'x' } }`, after `{ p: { a: 'x', c: 'c' } }`, and the form's `c` input shows `c`. The same `allOf` at the root already gave `c`.
- **A `$ref` spelled `#/definitions/%41` is the same recursion as `#/definitions/A`.** With the `A` above: before `{ name: 'n', child: { name: 'n', child: { name: 'n' } } }`, after `{ name: 'n', child: { name: 'n' } }`. A chain of aliases stops at its first alias the same way: with `node = { properties: { n: { default: 'n' }, c: { $ref: A1 } } }`, `A1 = { $ref: A2 }`, `A2 = { $ref: node }` and a root `$ref` to `node`: before `{ n: 'n', c: { n: 'n', c: { n: 'n' } } }`, after `{ n: 'n', c: { n: 'n' } }`.
- **Without a `rootSchema`, the schema is its own root whatever the data.** A `$ref` resolved against the schema only while the form data was empty. With `{ definitions: { a: { type: 'string', default: 'x' } }, properties: { p: { $ref: '#/definitions/a' }, q: { type: 'string' } } }`: before, every call threw `Could not find a definition for #/definitions/a.`. After, it gives `{ p: 'x' }`, and `{ p: 'x', q: '1' }` for the data `{ q: '1' }`. A `$ref` in a `dependencies` entry that the data qualifies resolves too, where it threw.
