- **`CoreUiOptionsChecks`** (`@rjsf/core`): The `UiOptionsCheck` union describing `@rjsf/core`'s own built-in widgets, fields and `ui:options`. Pass it as `StrictUiSchema`'s `Checks` argument (union it with your own `Checks` to extend it further) - it isn't included automatically.
- **`IndexedEnumOptionType<S>`**, **`EnumOptionsGroupType<S>`**, **`GroupedEnumOptionsType<S>`**: the option/group shapes returned by `groupEnumOptions()`, described below.
- **`SlotComponent<P>`**: the component-slot type behind `Widget`, `Field` and every `TemplatesType` entry: a function or class component taking `P`, with its props checked bivariantly. See [`Widget` type](#widget-type-breaking-change).
- **`Bivariant<Args, R>`**: a callback whose parameters are checked bivariantly, the way React checks event handlers, so an implementation may declare narrower parameters than the callback's own. `SlotComponent` and the `onChange` props are built from it, and the `CompiledValidateFunction` of `@rjsf/validator-ajv8` and of `@rjsf/validator-ata` extends it.
- **`ThemedForm<T, S, F>`** (`@rjsf/core`): what `withTheme()` returns. See [`withTheme()` returns a `ThemedForm`](#withtheme-returns-a-themedform-breaking-change).
- **`EventFormData<T>`** (`@rjsf/core`): the type of `IChangeEvent.formData` and `FormRef.getFormData()`. See [Generic defaults are `unknown` instead of `any`](#generic-defaults-are-unknown-instead-of-any-breaking-change).
- **`LogOnceLevel`**: `'warn' | 'error'`, the console method `logOnce()` logs through, described below.
- **`SchemaContext<S, F>`**: the `validator`, `customMergeAllOf` and `defaultFormStateBehavior` every validator-based schema function takes as its first parameter. See [`@rjsf/utils` schema functions take a `SchemaContext` in place of the `validator`](#rjsfutils-schema-functions-take-a-schemacontext-in-place-of-the-validator-breaking-change).
- **`GetDefaultFormStateProps<T, S, F>`**: the props object `getDefaultFormState()` takes after the `SchemaContext`.
- **`PresentationDeclaration`**, **`ThemePresentation`**, **`FieldPresentation`**: the presentation metadata proposed in [#5490](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5490), which `@rjsf/core` and every theme read. A `PresentationDeclaration` names who draws each part of a field (a `PresentationPart`: its `label`, `description`, `errors` and `help`) as a `PartOwner`: `'template'`, `'self'` or, for a field that renders a single widget, `'widget'`, plus `labelledBy`, whether a label the template draws reaches the control through its `for` or through the control's `aria-labelledby`. A widget's declarations, a `ThemePresentation`'s `widgets` and `default`, are `WidgetPresentationDeclaration`s, which can't name the `'widget'` owner; its `default` and `fieldDefault` take no `uncheckedIsFalse`, which is declared under the key of the one widget it describes. A `ThemePresentation` holds declarations by registry key under `widgets` and `fields`, plus `default` for the parts of every widget that the layer's own entry for it doesn't declare, custom and unregistered widgets included, and `fieldDefault` for the parts of every field that its merged entry doesn't declare, custom fields included, which is also what a part a field leaves to its widget falls back to when it renders none; `mergeThemePresentation()` merges a form's layers into a `MergedThemePresentation`. A `FieldPresentation` is one field's resolved plan: each part as a `PartPresentation`, placed `'template'`, `'self'`, `'above'` (drawn by a field above rendering the same id) or `'none'` (a `PartPlacement`) under the id it renders with, the `describedBy` ids of its parts that render, which a field below rendering the same id inherits, the `showLabel` its `ui:label` resolved to, its label's `requiredMarker`, planned for a container's heading too and carried by an `above` label from the label drawn above, its `control`'s `ControlAttributes`, and the `inherited` plan of the field above rendering the same id. `ResolvedPresentationDeclaration` is what `getPresentationDeclaration()` returns, with every part settled on `'template'` or `'self'`. Nothing is declared on a component: one given directly, as a `ui:field` or `ui:widget`, takes the declaration for the first key it is registered under that declares it.
- **`PresentationSource<T, S, F>`**, **`PresentationSubject<T, S, F>`**, **`FieldPresentationInputs`**, **`FieldLabelSources<S>`**: the parameter types of `getPresentationDeclaration()`, `resolveFieldPresentation()` and `getFieldLabel()`, described below.

### Type-safe UiSchema (opt-in)

`StrictUiSchema<Checks, T, S, F>` is a new, opt-in form of `UiSchema`: pass it a `Checks` union and it narrows `ui:widget`/`ui:field`/`ui:options` to only the values valid for each field's form-data type. An inline `uiSchema` JSX literal gets this narrowing too, by checking it with TypeScript's `satisfies` operator - no wrapper component needed.

`UiSchema` is otherwise unchanged - using `StrictUiSchema` is entirely opt-in and can be done incrementally, field by field or form by form.

See [Type-safe UiSchema](../api-reference/uiSchema.md#type-safe-uischema) for the full documentation, including examples, how to extend the widget/option vocabulary via `Checks`, and the current known gaps.

### New non-validator utility functions

- **`allowsAdditionalProperties<S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema, F extends FormContextType = FormContextType>(context: SchemaContext<S, F>, schema: S): boolean`**: returns whether an object takes keys its own `properties` don't name. A `patternProperties` naming a pattern says it does, whatever an `additionalProperties: false` beside it says about the names no pattern matches; otherwise the keyword describing those names answers, as long as it does not reject them, and a missing one takes them only where the `context`'s `additionalPropertiesWhenAbsent` is `'allow'`. `retrieveSchema()` stubs the extra keys the form data holds only for such an object, `canExpand()` offers the add button only for one, and `ObjectField` adds a property only to one, so the three no longer answer that question three ways ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330)).
- **`errorSchemaChild(key: string, value: unknown): Record<string, unknown> | unknown[] | undefined`**: returns `value` when a walk of an `ErrorSchema` descends into it: a plain object, or an array holding the errors of an array field's items. A value under `__errors` holds messages, so it is never a child. `toErrorList()`, `validationDataMerge()`, `unwrapErrorHandler()` and core's raised errors all walk by it ([#3827](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3827)).
- **`fieldPathEndsWithIndex(fieldPath)`**: reports whether a `FieldPath` addresses an array element.
- **`fieldPathToId(fieldPath, globalFormOptions)`**: derives the DOM id from a `FieldPath` using `idPrefix` and `idSeparator`.
- **`fieldPathToList(fieldPath)`**: parses a `FieldPath` back into a `FieldPathList`, with array indexes as numbers.
- **`fieldPathToName(fieldPath, globalFormOptions, isMultiValue?)`**: derives the HTML `name` from a `FieldPath` using the configured `nameGenerator`.
- **`flattenGroupedOptions(groupedOptions)`**: flattens the list `groupEnumOptions()` returns back into a plain list of options, in render order. For widgets that need every option in one flat collection (e.g. for value lookup) while still rendering the grouped structure.
- **`getAdditionalPropertySchema<S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema>(schema: S, key: string): S | boolean`**: returns the schema an object says applies to a `key` its own `properties` don't name. A key one or more `patternProperties` patterns match is described by all of them together, returned as an `allOf` for the caller to resolve, and a `false` among them rejects the key whatever the others allow. A key no pattern matches is `additionalProperties`' to describe, and an `unevaluatedProperties` answers for it only where the object names no `additionalProperties` at all, since any `additionalProperties` evaluates those keys itself. Either keyword spelled `undefined` reads as absent, the way a validator reads it. `false` for a key the object forbids and `true` for one it allows without describing. `retrieveSchema()` reads it to stub the field such a key renders with and `ObjectField` reads it to seed the property the add button creates, so the two cannot read a schema's prohibition, or a key's subschema, differently ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330)).
- **`getAdditionalPropertyType<S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema>(subSchema: S, rootSchema?: S): string | undefined`**: returns the type an additional property described by `subSchema` holds, read through `getSchemaOwnTypes()`, or `undefined` for a schema that leaves the type to the value the property comes to hold. A typeless `enum` takes the type of its values, where `getSchemaType()` answers `string` for any of them and so would give an `enum` of numbers a string it rejects, and an `anyOf`/`oneOf` of options that agree on a type has that type whichever option is chosen. A subschema that says nothing about the type itself is followed through the `$ref` it is spelled as, given a `rootSchema` to look the definition up in, so options spelled as references agree the way inline ones do. `retrieveSchema()` stubs the property with it and `ObjectField` seeds a new one from it ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330)).
- **`getDateTimeLocalValue(schema, value)`**: reports whether `schema.format` is `iso-date-time` and, if so, strips a timezone offset from the value for display. Shared by the `DateTimeWidget`s in `@rjsf/core`, `@rjsf/antd`, `@rjsf/daisyui`, and `@rjsf/mantine`.
- **`getDeprecatedHandling<T, S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema, F extends FormContextType = FormContextType>(schema: S, uiOptions: UIOptionsType<T, S, F>): 'hide' | 'disable' | 'label' | undefined`**: resolves how a field has to render a schema marked `deprecated`: `hide` tells the `FieldTemplate` the field is hidden, `disable` disables it, and the `label` default appends the deprecation marker to the field's label, which the `FieldTemplate` and the widget below it both receive, as [`getFieldLabel()`](#getfieldlabel) computes it. `undefined` for a schema that is not deprecated, so a `ui:deprecatedHandling` cannot hide or disable a field on its own. `SchemaField` calls it for every field, and a custom field rendering its own `FieldTemplate` should call it rather than read the option directly, which is how the `label` default gets missed ([#5327](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5327)).
- **`getFieldClassNames<S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema>(schema: S, hasErrors: boolean, uiClassNames?: string): string`**: builds the `classNames` string a `FieldTemplate` receives: `rjsf-field`, the `rjsf-field-<type>` class `getSchemaType()` derives, `rjsf-field-error` while the field has errors to show, and `ui:classNames` appended last. `SchemaField` calls it for every field; a custom field rendering its own `FieldTemplate` should call it rather than build the list by hand. A schema with no derivable type is spelled `rjsf-field-undefined`, as it has been since 6.x; that spelling is this helper's to change, in one place for every field, and is not one to key a CSS rule off. See [A discriminated cell of a layout grid renders through `SchemaField`](#a-discriminated-cell-of-a-layout-grid-renders-through-schemafield-breaking-change) ([#5327](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5327)).
- **`getFieldLabel<T = unknown, S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema, F extends FormContextType = FormContextType>(schema: S, uiOptions: UIOptionsType<T, S, F>, translateString: Registry<T, S, F>['translateString'], sources?: FieldLabelSources<S>): string`**: computes a field's label once, for its template and its widget alike: the first non-empty one of the `ui:title`, the unresolved schema's `title` (the one beside a `$ref`), the resolved schema's `title`, the `title` prop and the property name, so an empty `title` falls through to the name ([#5394](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5394)). It is the label the `FieldTemplate` shows and the one the field hands its widget, through the field's plan, so an array item whose `items` schema has a `title` is labelled by it in the themes whose widgets draw the label (mui, mantine, chakra-ui, fluentui-rc), rather than by the numbered title (`Tags-1`) `ArrayField` hands each item, where `StringField` and `NumberField` used to hand their widget the `title` prop first. An `additionalProperties` entry, or one whose key the user renamed, is labelled by its key; the heading an object entry draws itself still shows its `ui:title` where it has one. A deprecated field's label is decorated with the `DeprecatedLabel` marker, but only a label there is, so a field with no title and no name stays unlabelled rather than reading `(deprecated)` alone. The label a widget receives carries the marker too, in those same themes and for every checkbox, so a deprecated field whose widget draws its label shows the marker, where it used to show none.
- **`getKnownTypes<S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema>(schema: S): JSONSchema7TypeName[]`**: Returns the JSON Schema types a schema lists, dropping unknown names and duplicates, however many are left; an empty array for a schema whose `type` is a single name, since a name of its own is not a list to choose from. This is the list `getUnionTypes()` narrows to the schemas allowing more than one type, and the first answer `getSchemaOwnTypes()` gives, so a schema that lists only one recognized type alongside names no field can render allows that one type and renders as it
- **`getNumericInputTitle(inputProps, translateString)`**: returns the `title` for the numeric text input a `number`/`integer` field now defaults to — `TranslatableString.NumberInputTitle` ("Enter a number") or `TranslatableString.IntegerInputTitle` ("Enter a whole number"), localized through the given `translateString` — or `undefined` when `getInputProps()` set no `pattern` for the browser to reject a value against. Called by the `BaseInputTemplate` of every theme that renders a plain native `<input>`; call it from your own if you replace that template.
- **`getOptionalDataControlsType<S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema>(schema: S): string | string[] | undefined`**: returns the type the Optional Data Controls UI checks against `enableOptionalDataFieldForType`: an `anyOf`/`oneOf` schema's type is read from its options, any other schema's from its own `type`. `shouldRenderOptionalField()` and `OptionalDataControlsField` both use it, so they agree on it.
- **`getPresentationDeclaration<T = unknown, S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema, F extends FormContextType = FormContextType>(source: PresentationSource<T, S, F>, subject: PresentationSubject<T, S, F>): ResolvedPresentationDeclaration`**: resolves which parts of a field its template draws and which the components rendering it draw themselves, from the form's merged presentation, by the key each component is registered under; a component no key named, or named by a key that declares nothing, takes the declaration of the first key it is registered under that declares it, and a widget component that isn't registered takes the merged `default`. A field draws its own label and description unless its key or the merged `fieldDefault` declares otherwise, however `SchemaField` chose it; a field that renders a single widget declares `WIDGET_FIELD_PRESENTATION` to leave every part to that widget, and falls back to that field default when it renders none. The `widget` it is given is the one the field renders after its own defaulting, so a `BooleanField` given no widget or a `null` one passes `'checkbox'`. A widget `getWidget()` rejects, such as a name that is neither registered nor an alias, gets whatever is declared for it, and it never throws where `getWidget()` would.
- **`getRenderedOptionSchema<S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema>(parent: S, option: S): S`**: returns the schema an `anyOf`/`oneOf` option of `parent` is rendered with: an object parent passes on only its `required` list and its type, and any other parent every keyword that describes the value, leaving the options, the annotations, the `default` and the schema document keywords on the parent. A bound both declare, such as `maxItems` or `minimum`, keeps the stricter of the two, an `enum` is the option's list as it stands, an option's `true` subschema leaves the parent's in place, a tuple included, and any other keyword both declare that can't be combined into one value is the option's. `MultiSchemaField` renders the option with it, and `getUiRequiredErrorSchema()` and the defaults `getDefaultFormState()` computes for a field that is not an object read the option through it, so they agree on what was rendered. See [A non-object field's `anyOf`/`oneOf` option renders with its parent's keywords](#a-non-object-fields-anyofoneof-option-renders-with-its-parents-keywords-breaking-change) ([#5359](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5359)).
- **`getSchemaOwnTypes<S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema>(schema: S): JSONSchema7TypeName[] | undefined`**: returns the JSON Schema types a schema says its value has, taking the first answer of: the recognized names of a `type` list, as `getKnownTypes()` returns them; the types the values of a typeless `enum` or `const` have, read from the values rather than through `getSchemaType()`, which answers `string` for any typeless `enum`; and otherwise the type `getSchemaType()` resolves, so a schema that only implies its type — `properties` implying `object` — says what every other reader of it already renders it as. `undefined` for a schema that says nothing about the type: one whose only `type` is a name JSON Schema does not define, or whose type was guessed from the form data rather than named (an unconstrained `additionalProperties` entry, marked with `GUESSED_TYPE_FLAG`). The fallback UI reads it both for the types its selector offers and for whether to offer one at all, and `SchemaField` reads it to decide whether a non-select `anyOf`/`oneOf` supplies the types instead ([#5333](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5333)).
- **`getSchemaTypeForValue<S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema>(schema: S, value?: unknown): string | undefined`**: returns the type of a schema that a given value has: a value of any type a `type` list names is read as its own type, a number as the list's `integer` when it names that but not `number`, and anything else, an `undefined` value included, gets what `getSchemaType()` returns. `omitExtraData()` and `getUiRequiredErrorSchema()` read a value's type with it, so a string held by a `['null', 'object', 'string']` is neither pruned nor walked as an object ([#5331](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5331))
- **`getSelectFieldType(types: readonly string[]): string`**: returns the type of the field that renders a select over values of the given types: the one non-null type JSON Schema defines that it names, or `string` when it names several or none, since `StringField`'s select maps each option back to its constant. `SchemaField` and `getFieldTypeForWidget()` read a select's type with it ([#5331](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5331))
- **`getStaticItemsUiSchema<T = unknown, S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema, F extends FormContextType = FormContextType>(uiSchema?: UiSchema<T, S, F>, index?: number): UiSchema<T, S, F> | undefined`**: Returns the static (non-function) form of `uiSchema.items` that applies at tuple position `index` — a single object applies uniformly, an array of per-position objects resolves to the entry at `index` (or `undefined` when no `index` is given), and the dynamic `(itemData, index, formContext) => UiSchema` function form always resolves to `undefined`, since callers computing array item defaults don't have an item's data, index, or form context available to call it with
- **`getTemplates<T = unknown, S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema, F extends FormContextType = FormContextType>(registry: Registry<T, S, F>, uiOptions?: UIOptionsType<T, S, F>): TemplatesType<T, S, F>`**: returns the registry's templates with each one `uiOptions` overrides resolved through `getTemplate()`, or the registry's own map when nothing is overridden. Unlike `getTemplate()`, an override for a template name the registry lacks is left out. Core and the themes destructure the templates they render from it, since React's `static-components` lint rule reports a component returned by a call during render ([#5384](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5384)).
- **`getUiRequiredErrorSchema<T = unknown, S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema, F extends FormContextType = FormContextType>(context: SchemaContext<S, F>, rootSchema: S, uiSchema: UiSchema<T, S, F> | undefined, formData: T | undefined, uiSchemaDefinitions?: UiSchemaDefinitions<S, F>, globalUiOptions?: GlobalUISchemaOptions, formContext?: F): ErrorSchema<T>`**: Walks `rootSchema` and `formData` the same way `SchemaField` resolves each node while rendering, returning an `ErrorSchema` with a required error for every `ui:required: true` field that's missing. Also available as `SchemaUtilsType.getUiRequiredErrorSchema(uiSchema, formData, uiSchemaDefinitions, globalUiOptions, formContext)`, which supplies the first two arguments from the `SchemaUtils` instance
- **`enumOptionValueLabel(value: unknown): string`**: the label `optionsList()` gives an option with no title of its own: its value, with a plain object or array spelled as its JSON rather than `[object Object]`, and any other value, and anything nested in it that JSON can't represent, spelled as `String()` writes it.
- **`enumOptionsDomValues(enumOptions, format = 'indexed')`**: encodes every option of `enumOptions` for its DOM value attribute in one pass over the list, replacing the removed `enumOptionValueEncoder()`, so a widget reads each option's value at its position. In the `realValue` format it keeps options whose `String()` is the same, such as `1` and `'1'`, apart, and `enumOptionValueDecoder()` and `enumOptionSelectedValue()` encode against the list the same way ([#5315](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5315)).
- **`enumOptionsIsDisabled(value: unknown, enumDisabled?: EnumValue[]): boolean`**: reports whether `ui:enumDisabled` lists an option's `value`, matching strictly. Shared by `groupEnumOptions()` and by the `CheckboxesWidget`s and `RadioWidget`s in `@rjsf/core` and every theme; `@rjsf/daisyui`'s now honor `ui:enumDisabled` ([#5408](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5408)).
- **`enumOptionsAutofocusIndex(enumOptions, enumDisabled?, autofocus?, checkedValue?)`**: the index of the option a `RadioWidget` or `CheckboxesWidget` focuses under `ui:autofocus`: a radio group's checked option, given its value, where it is enabled, else the first one `ui:enumDisabled` leaves enabled, or -1. Shared by `@rjsf/core`'s and every theme's radio and checkboxes widgets.
- **`getExampleSuggestions(schema)`**: returns the distinct strings a text input suggests from `examples` and `default`, leaving out `null`, `undefined`, object and array values. `SchemaExamples` renders no `<datalist>` when there are none, and every theme's `BaseInputTemplate` sets `list` and the examples `aria-describedby` only when there are some, passing them to `SchemaExamples` through its new optional `suggestions` prop ([#5315](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5315)). The same schema object gets the same array back each time, which every caller shares, so it is frozen and typed `readonly string[]`: copy it (`[...getExampleSuggestions(schema)]`) before a `.sort()` or `.push()`, which otherwise fails to compile, or throws a `TypeError` behind a cast.
- **`useOptionFocusHandlers(props)`**: hook that builds a `CheckboxesWidget`'s per-option `onFocus`/`onBlur` handlers, reporting the focused option's value by its position rather than by decoding its DOM `value`. Shared by the `CheckboxesWidget`s in `@rjsf/core` and every theme ([#5315](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5315)).
- **`getFieldTypeForWidget<S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema>(schema: S, widget: unknown): string | undefined`**: returns the type of the field `SchemaField` renders a schema with its `ui:widget` by: for a `type` list, the type `getWidgetType()` picks for a named widget, or for a select over the list the type `getSelectFieldType()` gives it; otherwise the type `getSchemaType()` resolves. A `format` picks no other type's field. `SchemaField`, `getDisplayLabel()`, `getDefaultFormState()`, `FallbackField`'s type selector and `ObjectField`'s new additional properties follow it ([#5331](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5331))
- **`getUnionTypes<S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema>(schema: S): JSONSchema7TypeName[] | undefined`**: Returns the types a schema allows when it allows more than one of them, i.e. its `type` is an array of two or more non-`null` type names, dropping unknown names and duplicates; `undefined` for anything `getSchemaType()` resolves to a single type, a nullable type included. Paired with the new `JSON_SCHEMA_TYPES` constant, the list of every JSON Schema type name, which is what the fallback UI offers for a schema that constrains the type not at all
- **`getVisibleErrors(props)`**: returns the errors a widget or template should surface in its own UI, given anything carrying `rawErrors` and `hideError` (its `VisibleErrorsProps` parameter type is exported too). The list is empty whenever `ui:hideError` is in effect, so a component can derive its error state from one call instead of pairing `rawErrors` with `hideError` itself. Deprecated, with `VisibleErrorsProps`, and kept through v7 for a component rendered without a plan, in favor of `getErrorsToDraw()`, which gives the plan's `presentation.errors.messages` only where `presentation.errors.by` is `'self'`; see [`ui:hideError` now suppresses the theme's error styling](#uihideerror-now-suppresses-the-themes-error-styling-breaking-change).
- **`getWidgetType<S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema>(schema: S, widget: string): string | undefined`**: returns the type whose field renders a widget, by its alias or registered name: the type the schema resolves to when it has the widget, otherwise, when that type is a `string`, `number`, `integer` or `boolean`, the first other one of those its `type` list names that does. `getWidget()` looks an alias up by it, and `SchemaField` renders that type's field, so a `textarea` on a `['null', 'number', 'string']` renders `StringField` and keeps what is typed as a string. A list resolving to `object` or `array` takes only its own widgets, and a select over a `type` list only those of the field `getSelectFieldType()` renders it through ([#5331](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5331))
- **`getXxxOfKey<S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema>(schema: S): 'anyOf' | 'oneOf' | undefined`**: returns the keyword whose options are rendered for a schema, `anyOf` when it carries both. Every reader of the options in `@rjsf/utils` and `@rjsf/core` goes through it, so a custom field that reads the options itself can too, and agree with the rest of the form on which list is on screen ([#5309](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5309)).
- **`getXxxOfOptions<S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema>(schema: S): { key: 'anyOf' | 'oneOf'; options: S[] } | undefined`**: returns the `anyOf`/`oneOf` options that are rendered for a schema along with the keyword they are read from, as `getXxxOfKey()` picks it, and `undefined` where there is no non-empty list. The companion to `getXxxOfKey()` for the readers that want the options themselves, so that reading them as `anyOf ?? oneOf` — which takes an empty `anyOf: []` over a `oneOf` that has options — is not left to each of them.
- **`groupEnumOptions(enumOptions, optgroups?, enumDisabled?)`**: groups `enumOptions` according to `ui:options.optgroups`, tagging every option with its original array index (needed for index-based value encoding) and its disabled status. Powers the `optgroups` support in every theme's `SelectWidget`, described above.
- **`hasVisibleErrors(props)`**: the boolean form of `getVisibleErrors()`, for the widgets and templates that only need to decide whether to render an error state rather than render the errors themselves. Deprecated, and kept through v7 for a component rendered without a plan, in favor of `hasWidgetErrors()` and `hasTemplateErrors()`, which fall back to it only without one.
- **`isComponentType<P = never>(value: unknown): value is ComponentType<P>`**: reports whether a value is a component React can render: a function, or a `memo()`, `forwardRef()` or `lazy()` component, but not a React element such as `<MyField />` ([#5363](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5363)).
- **`isConstantOptionList<S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema>(options: unknown, nonEmpty = false): options is S[]`**: reports whether an `anyOf`/`oneOf` list is made entirely of constant schemas (see `isConstant()`), the shape rendered as a select. An empty list passes, since every one of its options is vacuously a constant, unless `nonEmpty` is set for a caller that needs an option to exist.
- **`isConstantSelect<S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema>(schema: S, allowEmpty = false): boolean`**: reports whether a schema's value is picked from an `enum` or an `anyOf`/`oneOf` of constants (read from the keyword `getXxxOfKey()` picks), whatever its `type`. An empty list passes only with `allowEmpty`, which gives `isSelect()`'s answer without resolving the schema; otherwise an empty `enum` leaves the `anyOf`/`oneOf` to decide. An `object` or `array` schema that passes renders as one select for its whole value; see `isWholeValueSelect()`.
- **`isWholeValueSelect<S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema>(schema: S): boolean`**: reports whether a schema is a select over object or array constants, which holds one of them as a whole: a non-empty `isConstantSelect()` schema whose `type` is `object` or `array`, or which declares no `type` and offers an object or array constant. `getDisplayLabel()`, `getDefaultFormState()`, `omitExtraData()`, `sanitizeDataForNewSchema()`, `getWidget()` and the `ui:required` check all treat such a select's value as one constant rather than a container through it.
- **`isSchemaObject<S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema>(schemaDef: unknown): schemaDef is S`**: narrows a subschema, which JSON Schema also allows to be a boolean, and which can be absent, to a schema object ([#5331](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5331))
- **`isEnumOptionsGroup(item)`**: type guard distinguishing a group from a standalone option in the list `groupEnumOptions()` returns.
- **`localTimeToOffsetTime(time)`**: appends the browser's current local UTC offset to a bare `HH:MM`/`HH:MM:SS` time string, without altering the wall-clock value.
- **`logOnce(message: string, level: LogOnceLevel = 'warn', error?: unknown)`**: logs `message` (followed by `error`, when there is one) through `console.warn()`, or `console.error()` when `level` is `'error'`, but only the first time it is seen, so a warning raised while rendering isn't repeated on every re-render. What has been logged is remembered for the whole page. The warnings `@rjsf/utils`, `@rjsf/core` and the validators raise while rendering now go through it ([#3051](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/3051))
- **`mergeThemePresentation(...layers: (ThemePresentation | undefined)[]): MergedThemePresentation`**: merges the `ThemePresentation`s of a form's layers, from the bottom, part by part rather than replacing each declaration whole, so a theme declaring that its `CheckboxWidget` draws its errors keeps `@rjsf/core`'s declaration that it draws its label. A part declared `undefined` is left to the layers beneath it. Each layer's `default` is layered beneath that layer's own entry for every widget key any layer declares, so a theme's or the `Form` props' `default` overrides the parts it declares in the layers beneath it, even under a key one of them declares, and the layered `default`s are what a widget no layer declares draws. The `fieldDefault`s are layered over one another only, beneath the merged field entries, so a theme's can't override a part a lower layer declares under a key. Neither a `default` nor a `fieldDefault` keeps an `uncheckedIsFalse` its layer gives it anyway, as a theme written in JavaScript can, since it describes one control and is declared under that widget's key.
- **`offsetTimeToLocalTime(time)`**: strips a trailing timezone offset (`Z` or `+HH:MM`/`-HH:MM`) from a `time` string, for feeding back into a native time input.
- **`omitConsumedStyling<T = unknown, S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema, F extends FormContextType = FormContextType>(uiSchema?: UiSchema<T, S, F>): UiSchema<T, S, F> | undefined`**: returns the `uiSchema` to hand a field's children with the class names and style the field's own `FieldTemplate` consumed removed, in all four spellings (`ui:classNames`, a bare `classNames`, `ui:style`, and the `ui:options` equivalents), so a child cannot apply them a second time inside the wrapper already carrying them ([#439](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/439)). The original object comes back when there is nothing to strip, so a field memoizing it keeps the identity it had. This is the stripping `SchemaField` has always done inline ([#5327](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5327)).
- **`padTimeSeconds(value)`**: appends `:00` seconds to a bare `HH:MM` time or the time portion of a naive local date-time string that's missing them, since RFC 3339 requires seconds regardless of whether a timezone offset is present.
- **`rendersOwnFieldBesideOption<S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema>(schema: S): boolean`**: whether the field of `schema` renders a field of its own beside its selected `anyOf`/`oneOf` option, as an object's `ObjectField` does. `getRenderedOptionSchema()`, the defaults `getDefaultFormState()` computes, the `getUiRequiredErrorSchema()` walk and `MultiSchemaField` all read it to choose between the option as rendered and the option merged with the whole field ([#5359](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5359)).
- **`replaceEqualDeep(prev, next)`**: returns `next` with every subtree deeply equal to `prev` replaced by the `prev` instance, so reference-based memoization sees unchanged data as unchanged. `Form` applies it to the state it builds for a change.
- **`resetLogOnce()`**: forgets what `logOnce()` has logged. What has been logged is remembered for the whole page, so if your tests assert on an RJSF warning, call this before each test (e.g. `beforeEach(resetLogOnce)` in your Vitest or Jest setup file); otherwise only the first test to trigger a warning will see it logged
- **`resolveFieldPresentation(inputs: FieldPresentationInputs): FieldPresentation`**: builds a field's plan of what draws its label, description, help and errors, under which ids, and the `aria-describedby`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-label` (the name of a control whose label `ui:label: false` hides), `aria-invalid` and `required` its control carries and whether its label shows a required marker, from the declaration `getPresentationDeclaration()` resolved and the field's own text. `ui:label: false` hides the description along with the label. Under the plan of a field above rendering the same id (`inherited`), a part any field above already shows is `'above'`, text of the field's own takes `optionDescriptionId()` or `optionHelpId()` when a field above shows text under the id it would take, numbered by how many fields above show text of their own, a label of its own with a control to name takes `optionLabelId()` when a field above draws a label under the id it would take, and the errors are the field above's, which describe the control and mark it `aria-invalid`. A `componentId` gives the parts the field's own component draws ids of their own, for a field rendered beside its `anyOf`/`oneOf` options under the `XxxOf` id.
- **`getControlAttributes(props, ownDescribedBy?)`**: the `aria-labelledby`, `aria-label` (the name of a control whose label `ui:label: false` hides), `aria-describedby` and `aria-invalid` a widget spreads onto the element carrying its `id`, from its field's plan, followed by the ids of elements the widget renders to describe its control itself, such as its examples list. A widget rendered without a plan gets `ariaDescribedByIds(id)`. The plan's `required` is left to the widget's own `required` prop.
- **`getControlDescribedBy(props, ownDescribedBy?)`**: the `aria-describedby` alone of what `getControlAttributes()` gives a widget, for a widget that needs only that attribute.
- **`getSelectorPresentation(field, selectorId)`**: the plan of an `anyOf`/`oneOf` field's option selector, derived from the field's: it draws the parts the field draws itself, and is named through `aria-labelledby` by a label the template draws and described by the field's parts that render.
- **`getDescribedControlAttributes(describedBy, errors)`**: the `aria-describedby` of the parts that render and the `aria-invalid` while errors show, as `resolveFieldPresentation()` gives a control, for a control the resolver didn't plan.
- **`getWidgetDescription(props)`**: the text and id of the description a widget draws itself, as a checkbox does: the one its field's plan says the widget draws, or, without a plan, its `ui:description` or schema `description` unless `hideLabel` is set. Without a plan it can't tell that `@rjsf/core`'s and `@rjsf/chakra-ui`'s `FieldTemplate`s draw the description of a field whose `ui:widget` is the `checkbox` alias ([#4825](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/4825)), so a checkbox widget in those themes doesn't call it there.
- **`isLabelledByTemplate(presentation)`**: whether the field's label is drawn outside its component, by its template or `above` it by a field above rendering the same id, whoever draws it there.
- **`isNamedByTemplateLabel(props)`**: whether a label the `FieldTemplate` draws names a widget's control: `isLabelledByTemplate()` under a plan, or, without one, a label shown with text.
- **`getControlFallbackName(props, prompt?)`**: the field's label, else its `placeholder`, else the translated `prompt` (the select prompt by default), which a control that takes no name from what it displays, such as a combobox trigger, takes as its `aria-label` where no label names it.
- **`getControlAriaLabel(props, ariaLabel?, drawsOwnLabel?)`**: the `aria-label` of such a control: the caller's or the plan's `aria-label`, else none where a label the widget draws or one the template draws names it, else `getControlFallbackName()`.
- **`widgetDrawsLabelText(props)`**: whether a widget draws a label of its own with text in it, where `widgetDrawsLabel()` says it draws its field's label and that label has text, so it draws no label holding only a required field's `*`.
- **`getFieldTypeForUiSchema(schema, uiSchema?)`**: the type of the field `SchemaField` renders a schema by, for the field's own `ui:widget`.
- **`getGroupAttributes(props, role, drawnLabelId?)`**: the attributes `getControlAttributes()` gives a widget rendering a group of controls, such as radios or checkboxes, split into the `role` and `aria-labelledby` for the group's wrapper, since a label's `for` reaches a single control, or the `aria-label` its plan gives a group whose label `ui:label: false` hides, and the `aria-describedby` for each control in it. `aria-invalid` goes on a `'radiogroup'` rather than its radios, and on each control of a `'group'`, such as its checkboxes, where ARIA supports it; the wrapper taking the `role` keeps the two together. A widget that draws its own label for the group passes that label's id as `drawnLabelId`, which names the group where its plan gives the widget the label and names the group by no other.
- **`getGroupLabelling(props, role)`**: what a widget rendering a group of controls that draws its own label for the group needs: whether it draws one, which it does where `widgetDrawsLabel()` says so and there is label text, the id of the field's label from `getLabelId()`, which it draws it under, and the `group` and `option` attributes `getGroupAttributes()` gives, with the group named by that label where its plan names it by no other.
- **`getLabelId(props)`**: the id of a field's label, which its plan gives it whoever draws it, numbered with `optionLabelId()` beneath a label a field above draws, or `fieldLabelId(id)` without a plan. A widget that draws its own label draws it under this id.
- **`templateDrawsLabel(props)`**, **`templateDrawsDescription(props)`**, **`hasTemplateErrors(props)`**, **`widgetDrawsLabel(props)`** and **`hasWidgetErrors(props)`**: whether a `FieldTemplate` draws its field's label, whether it draws the description it is handed (where it draws the label, without a plan), whether it has errors to show, whether a widget draws its own label, and whether a widget's control has errors to show, read from the field's plan, falling back to the deprecated `displayLabel`, `rawErrors` with `hideError`, and `hideLabel`, and, for a widget's errors, its `rawErrors` with `hideError`, only for a template or widget rendered without one.
- **`getErrorsToDraw(props)`**: the error messages a widget, or an `ArrayFieldTemplate` or `ObjectFieldTemplate`, draws itself: its field's plan's `presentation.errors.messages`, with `ui:hideError` applied, where `presentation.errors.by` is `'self'`, and none where the `FieldTemplate` or a field above draws them, falling back to `rawErrors` with `hideError` only for a component rendered without a plan. Where `hasWidgetErrors()` and `hasTemplateErrors()` give the error state, it gives the messages, for a theme whose widgets or container templates draw their own, as `@rjsf/mantine`'s do ([#5325](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5325)).
- **`templateDrawsPlannedLabel(props)`**: whether a `FieldTemplate` in a theme whose widgets draw their own label draws the field's label: only where the field's plan gives the label to the template and there is label text to draw. Unlike `templateDrawsLabel()`, it draws none for a template rendered without a plan, whose widgets then draw their own label.
- **`titleDrawsOptionalDataControl(props)`**: whether an `ArrayFieldTemplate` or `ObjectFieldTemplate` lays out its optional data control in the heading it draws for its `title`. A readonly or disabled container, and one drawing no heading, as under `ui:label: false` or where a field above draws the text, lays it out with its items or properties instead, so a custom container template doesn't drop the control with the heading.
- **`templateLabelFor(props)`**: the `for` of the label a `FieldTemplate` draws: the field's `id`, except where the field's plan names the control through `aria-labelledby`, as for a group of radios or checkboxes, whose wrapper carries the `id` and isn't an element a `for` can label, so a custom `FieldTemplate` doesn't point its label at that wrapper.
- **`templateMarksRequired(props)`**: whether a `FieldTemplate` marks the label it draws as required: where the field's plan says so, which leaves the marker off a control declared `uncheckedIsFalse` whose schema doesn't demand `true`, or, without a plan, where the field is `required`, so a custom `FieldTemplate` marks its label as `@rjsf/core`'s does.
- **`getArrayDescription(props)`**: the description an `ArrayFieldTemplate` draws: the one its plan says the array draws itself, so an option repeating the description a field above draws doesn't draw it again, or, without a plan, its `ui:description` or schema `description`.
- **`hasAdditionalKeyLabel(props)`**: whether a `WrapIfAdditionalTemplate` labels the key input of an `additionalProperties` entry, which it does wherever the entry's value is labelled, by the template or by the value's own component; without a plan it follows `displayLabel`.
- **`resolveWidget<T = unknown, S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema, F extends FormContextType = FormContextType>(schema: RJSFSchema, widget?: Widget<T, S, F> | string, registeredWidgets?: RegistryWidgetsType<T, S, F>): { Widget: Widget<T, S, F> }`**: returns `getWidget()`'s widget as `{ Widget }`, for the same reason: a component destructured from it passes the `static-components` rule ([#5384](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5384)).
- **`schemaKey<S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema>(schema: S): string`**: the key a validator caches a schema's compiled validation function under, and the key `getValidationSchemas()` maps it into the compiled set under: the schema's `$id` when it names something, and `hashForSchema(schema)` otherwise. An empty `$id` names nothing, so a schema carrying one is keyed by its hash rather than sharing the empty key with every other such schema. `@rjsf/validator-ajv8`, `@rjsf/validator-ata` and `@rjsf/validator-cfworker` had each spelled this rule out for themselves and had drifted apart on the empty `$id`; they all call this now. Two schemas sharing a key must validate the same way, so a schema derived from another in a way that changes its meaning carries an `$id` derived from its own content instead — if you write a validator or derive schemas to validate against, use this for the cache key rather than reading `$id` directly ([#5338](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5338)).
- **`toFieldPath(segment, parentPath?)`**: appends a property name or array index to a parent `FieldPath`. `ROOT_FIELD_PATH` is the path of the root form.
- **`useSelectFocusHandlers(props)`**: hook that builds a select widget's `onFocus`/`onBlur` handlers, reporting its form data `value` as it is, or the `emptyValue` option when nothing is selected. Shared by the `SelectWidget`s in `@rjsf/antd`, `@rjsf/chakra-ui`, `@rjsf/daisyui`, `@rjsf/fluentui-rc`, `@rjsf/mantine`, `@rjsf/mui` and `@rjsf/shadcn`, whose focused element carries no option value to decode.
- **`useTimeWidgetProps(props)`**: hook that combines `localTimeToOffsetTime()`, `offsetTimeToLocalTime()` and `padTimeSeconds()` into a `TimeWidget`'s offset-free display value and a `computeTimeValue()` transform for its `onChange` handler. Shared by the `TimeWidget`s in `@rjsf/core`, `@rjsf/daisyui`, and `@rjsf/mantine`.
- **`getValidationSchemas<S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema>(rootSchema: S): SchemaMap<S>`**: every schema a form hands its validator for `rootSchema`, keyed by `schemaKey()` and carrying that key as its `$id`: the root, a `$ref` to each `if`, option and discriminator property, the copy of an option scoring derives, each dependency condition, and `{}` where an option is a boolean, with `{ not: {} }` where it is `false`. It is what the precompiled validator packages compile. See [Validators are handed pointers into the root schema](#validators-are-handed-pointers-into-the-root-schema-breaking-change).
- **`getValidationTarget<S extends StrictRJSFSchema = RJSFSchema>(schema: S, rootSchema: S): S`**: the `{ $ref }` naming where `schema` sits in `rootSchema`, a schema `retrieveSchema()` handed out included, or, for a schema with no place there, the schema itself, an `$id` it carries replaced by `<that $id>?rjsf=<its own hash>` so a validator compiles it in its own right. Hand it to `isValid()` to check data against a subschema as JSON Schema reads it, or a schema you derived from one carrying an `$id`.
- **`dateElementId(rootId, type)`**: the id `DateElement` gives each of the date element selectors an `AltDateWidget` renders, e.g. `dateElementId('root_birthday', 'year')` is `root_birthday_year`. A theme's `AltDateWidget` uses it to point a label at the year, month or day control instead of rebuilding that id itself; the separator is always `_`, independent of the form's `idSeparator`
- **`dateElementLabel(type, translateString)`**: the translated name of a date element an `AltDateWidget` renders, e.g. `year`, through its `TranslatableString`; any other `type` is returned as it is
- **`dateElementAriaLabel(elementLabel, translateString, label?)`**: the accessible name `DateElement` gives a date element, the field's `label` and `dateElementLabel()` combined through `TranslatableString.DateElementAriaLabel`, e.g. `When, year`, or the element's name alone when the label is empty
- **`widgetAriaProps(props)`**: the `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby` and `aria-invalid` a `SelectWidget` gives the element a screen reader focuses. Under its field's plan, those its plan gives the control, with the ids its caller passed and then `selectedOptionDescriptionId(id)`, while the selected option's description renders, added to the plan's describers, and the caller's `aria-label` over the plan's unless it is empty. Without a plan, the caller's ids, or `ariaDescribedByIds(id)` when it passed none, followed by `descriptionId(id)` while that description renders. A `SelectWidget` should give the whole result to its control, so it keeps whatever the plan adds
- **`fieldLabelId(id)`**: the id for the label a theme's `FieldTemplate` renders above a field's control, e.g. `root_birthday__label`. A widget that a `label htmlFor` cannot name — a group of controls, since the association reaches a single control rather than the element wrapping them — points `aria-labelledby` at it, so its accessible name is the text on the screen. A single control, a picker's `button` included, is named by the label's `htmlFor` alone and should not point here, since `aria-labelledby` outranks that association and resolves to nothing under a `FieldTemplate` that renders no such label. It cannot be `titleId()`, which `TitleFieldTemplate` already claims for the same field
- **`optionDescriptionId(id, depth = 1)`**, **`optionHelpId(id, depth = 1)`** and **`optionLabelId(id, depth = 1)`**: the ids for the description, help and label a selected `anyOf`/`oneOf` option or the fallback UI's value field renders of its own, beneath a field that renders different text under `descriptionId()` or `helpId()` for the same id, or whose own component draws a label under `fieldLabelId()`, e.g. `root_pet__optionDescription`. `depth` counts the fields above that show text of their own, so an option of an option takes `root_pet__optionDescription2`. `@rjsf/core` doesn't render under these ids yet.
- **`selectedOptionDescriptionId(id)`**: the id for the description of the option a select widget has selected, which describes that option rather than the field and so can't share its `descriptionId()`, e.g. `root_pet__selectedOptionDescription`. `SelectedOptionDescription` renders under it when its field's plan reaches it, and under `descriptionId()` without one.
- **`callWithDeferredThrow(callback)`**: calls a consumer's callback and rethrows anything it throws from a `setTimeout()` instead of to the caller. A widget or field that calls one from an Effect or its cleanup uses it so the throw does not unmount the form; the throw then escapes error boundaries and is reported as an uncaught error
- **`setOwn(obj: GenericObjectType, key: string | number, value: unknown)`**: writes `value` at `key` of a plain object as an own, enumerable property, defining a `__proto__` key rather than assigning it, so JSON-sourced data holding one keeps it as a key instead of re-pointing the object's prototype. `setByPath()` writes every key through it ([#5561](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5561))

`@rjsf/utils` also exports the `WIDGET_FIELD_PRESENTATION` constant, the `PresentationDeclaration` of a field that leaves every part to the single widget it renders, and the `DEFAULT_BOOLEAN_WIDGET` constant, the registry key the `checkbox` alias of a `boolean` resolves to (`CheckboxWidget`), so a theme can read that widget from its registry without copying the key. `@rjsf/daisyui`'s `FieldTemplate` reads it to tell whether a field's widget renders its own label ([#5456](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/pull/5456))

### Changes to existing utility functions

- **`canExpand()`**: Takes a `SchemaContext` as its first parameter, whose `additionalPropertiesWhenAbsent` says whether an object naming no `additionalProperties` takes extra keys. Call `registry.schemaUtils.canExpand(schema, uiSchema, formData)` from a template. See [`canExpand()` and `allowsAdditionalProperties()` take a `SchemaContext`](#canexpand-and-allowsadditionalproperties-take-a-schemacontext-breaking-change)
- **`allowsAdditionalProperties()`**: Takes a `SchemaContext` as its first parameter, for the same reason, and is on `SchemaUtilsType` as `allowsAdditionalProperties(schema)`
- **`getRenderedOptionSchema()`**: The option of an object parent is marked with the new `EXTRA_KEYS_ELSEWHERE_FLAG` Symbol, beside the `required` list and type it already took, so its own field offers no extra key beside the parent's even where the form reads an absent `additionalProperties` as taking any key
- **`schemaParser()`**: Deprecated. It is `getValidationSchemas(rootSchema)`, which returns the schemas a form hands its validator, under the name 6.x exported it as, so it returns the new list and code calling it still has to regenerate its precompiled validators. Call `getValidationSchemas()` in its place. See [Validators are handed pointers into the root schema](#validators-are-handed-pointers-into-the-root-schema-breaking-change).
- **Every validator-based schema function**: Takes a `SchemaContext` in place of its `validator`, and no longer takes a `customMergeAllOf` or `defaultFormStateBehavior`. See [`@rjsf/utils` schema functions take a `SchemaContext` in place of the `validator`](#rjsfutils-schema-functions-take-a-schemacontext-in-place-of-the-validator-breaking-change)
- **`getDefaultFormState()`**: Takes a `SchemaContext` followed by a `GetDefaultFormStateProps` object, and added optional `uiSchema` and `uiSchemaDefinitions` props used to apply `ui:emptyValue` and `ui:initialValue` as defaults
  - These were added as optional trailing parameters of the `SchemaUtilsType` interface's version of `getDefaultFormState()` as well
- **`shouldRenderOptionalField()`**: Takes the field's `fieldPath` as a required fifth argument, and its fourth, `uiSchema`, is no longer optional: `shouldRenderOptionalField(registry, schema, required, uiSchema, fieldPath)`. A field of your own passes the `fieldPath` from its props; a JavaScript caller that still passes four arguments gets no error, and its root field renders behind an Add button, since a missing `fieldPath` is not the root's. It returns `false` for the root field, and no longer mistakes a nested field for it: the option a root `anyOf`/`oneOf` selects no longer gets the controls, while a nested object or array that a recursive `$ref: '#'` describes now does
- **`isRootSchema()`**: Removed. It compared a schema's content with the root schema's, which is what made `shouldRenderOptionalField()` mistake a nested field for the root. Compare the field's `fieldPath` with `ROOT_FIELD_PATH` instead: `fieldPath === ROOT_FIELD_PATH`
- **`findSchemaDefinition()`**: Deprecated, and resolves its `$ref` through the schema graph, as the form does. A `$ref` to an `$anchor` resolves where it threw; the keywords beside a `$ref` the target holds read as the `allOf` of both, where draft-07 read them as overrides; the result is marked with `RJSF_REF_KEY` and shared across calls, so copy it rather than changing it; and the `baseURI` parameter the v7 branch had added is gone. It throws the same `Could not find a definition for ...` error for a `$ref` that resolves to nothing. Resolve a `$ref` the way the form does with `schemaUtils.retrieveSchema({ $ref })` instead
- **`getOptionMatchingSimpleDiscriminator()`**: Removed. It read the discriminator property only from an option's own `properties`, so for the options `[{ $ref: '#/definitions/A' }, { $ref: '#/definitions/B' }]` whose `kind` is `const` `'a'` and `'b'`, the data `{ kind: 'b' }` and the discriminator `kind` it returned `undefined`. Call `getFirstMatchingOption()` or `getClosestMatchingOption()` with the `discriminatorField`, which read it through the option's `$ref`, its `allOf` entries and the rest of its scope, and return `1` there.
- **`relaxOptionsForScoring()`**: Removed. `omitExtraData()` relaxes the whole scope of an option itself, from where the option sits in the root. To score options with their `additionalProperties: false` relaxed yourself, hand `getClosestMatchingOption()` or `getFirstMatchingOption()` copies of them with `additionalProperties: true`.
- **`schemaHasNestedConditional()`**: Removed. `Form` no longer compares root-level retrieved schemas to decide whether to sanitize: it compares the field trees before and after a change, which reach a `dependencies` or `if` at any depth (see [Sanitize is a diff of the field trees](#sanitize-is-a-diff-of-the-field-trees-breaking-change)). Nothing replaces it
- **`getSchemaType()`**: A schema without a `type` whose `const` is falsy (`0`, `false`, `''` or `null`) now reports the constant's type instead of `undefined`. See [A typeless `oneOf`/`anyOf` of constants now renders as a select](#a-typeless-oneofanyof-of-constants-now-renders-as-a-select-breaking-change) for what this changes. A `type` list of three or more entries that starts with `null`, such as `['null', 'string', 'number']`, now resolves to its first type other than `null` rather than to `null`, as a two-entry nullable list already did, so it renders that type's field rather than `NullField` and `getDefaultFormState()` fills it in as that type: a required list whose first type other than `null` is `boolean` starts at `false`, and one whose first is `object` or `array` gets that type's computed defaults. Of the types any list names, it prefers one JSON Schema defines, so a `['null', 'foo', 'string']` resolves to `string`, and a `['foo', 'string']` to `string` rather than `foo`. Its return type narrows from `string | string[] | undefined` to `string | undefined`, since it never returned an array, so a caller's `Array.isArray()` check on it can go ([#5331](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5331))
- **`enumOptionSelectedValue()`**: In the `realValue` format the selected value is encoded the same way `enumOptionsDomValues()` encodes the options' values, instead of with `String()`, so a selected object or `null` value now matches its option. It also no longer reports "no selection" purely because the value equals the `emptyValue` a widget passed it, so an option carrying that widget's `null` or `''` sentinel shows as selected. An entry of a multiple selection that matches no option is left out, as the `indexed` format leaves it out
- **`enumOptionValueDecoder()`**: In the `realValue` format a string decodes to the option `enumOptionsDomValues()` encodes as that string, and no longer falls back to the option at a bare index no option encodes as. That fallback returned the wrong option whenever a number option shared its value with another option's position, e.g. `'1'` decoded to `1` rather than `5` for the options `1` and `5`. A widget that holds an option's position resolves it with `enumOptionsValueForIndex()` instead.
- **`enumOptionsIsSelected()`**: Takes an optional third `multiple` argument. A single-select widget passes `false`, so an array `selected` is compared as the whole value of an array option such as `{ "const": [2] }` rather than as a list of selections; omitted, an array is still a list. `enumOptionsIndexForValue()` prefers an option matching the whole value when not `multiple`, which fixes `enumOptionSelectedValue()` for array constants too, and the radio widgets pass `false`
- **`enumOptionsSelectValue()`**: Compares values by deep equality and accepts a `null` option, so an object option selected in a checkbox list lands in the options' order, whether or not the form data holds the option's own instance It no longer adds a value that is already selected, and keeps a selected value that matches no option after the ones that do
- **`getDisplayLabel()`**: An `object` or `array` schema whose `enum` or `anyOf`/`oneOf` is a non-empty list of constants now displays its label, since it renders as one select for the whole value rather than as the fields of its contents
- **`getWidget()`**: The `object` and `null` types now accept the `select`, `radio` and `hidden` widget names, which a select over object constants, or one whose `type` is `null`, renders with, and an `array` select over whole array constants accepts `select`, `radio` and `hidden`, throwing for `checkboxes` and `files`, which read the picked array as a list of selections. A multi-select array still throws for `radio`, since a radio group would write one value in place of its list. Only the aliases are checked: a widget named by its registered name, such as `CheckboxesWidget`, renders as named. Asking for any other name for an `object` or `null` type now throws `No widget '<name>' for type '<type>'` instead of `No widget for type '<type>'`. A widget that isn't a component, such as a React element or a context's `Provider` or `Consumer`, now throws `Unsupported widget definition`. A schema whose `type` is a list looks a widget name up by the type `getWidgetType()` picks, so a `textarea` on a `['null', 'number', 'string']` renders `TextareaWidget` rather than throwing, while a `null` type's `radio` on a multi-select `['array', 'null']` still throws and an `array` widget such as `checkboxes` or `files` still needs the list to resolve to `array`. A select over a `type` list looks its widget up by the field `getSelectFieldType()` renders it through, so a `checkbox` on an `enum` of `['a', true]` throws `No widget 'checkbox' for type 'string'` ([#5331](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5331))
- **`isSelect()`**: Reads `anyOf` before `oneOf`, the same as `optionsList()`, so a schema carrying both keywords is judged by the list that renders ([#5309](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5309)). `sanitizeDataForNewSchema()` and `findFieldInSchema()` read `anyOf` first too, and `omitExtraData()` checks the `oneOf`/`anyOf` list it prunes by rather than asking `isSelect()` about the parent
- **`optionsList()`**: Returns `undefined` instead of throwing when the `anyOf`/`oneOf` it reads is not made of constants and no selector field names where the values are. An option with no title of its own and an object or array value is labelled with its JSON instead of `[object Object]`. A selector property is read by own key, so an option that doesn't declare one named like an `Object.prototype` member, such as `toString`, is read by its constant rather than through the inherited function ([#5561](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5561))
- **`retrieveSchema()`**: The stub it builds for a property described only by an `additionalProperties` with no `type` now keeps the rest of that subschema instead of discarding it, so a property constrained by an `enum`, a `format`, a length or a range renders as a field that honors it rather than as free text. Subschema keywords such as `allOf`, and a `default` of another type than the data, are left out, as are the identifiers (`$id`, `$anchor`, ...) and containers (`$defs`, `definitions`) of a stub whose type the subschema only implies or the data supplies: such a stub is the form's own reading of what one property holds, and copying an `$id` onto every sibling property would route them all to a field registered under it. This is not gated behind `useFallbackUiForUnsupportedType`, so such a property renders as the field its constraint calls for in every form, and a value of another type than that constraint allows is as invisible in it as in any field for a schema that names a type. The stub still takes the type of the data the property holds, and is now marked with the new `GUESSED_TYPE_FLAG` symbol to record that the type was guessed rather than declared by the schema when the subschema asserts nothing about the value. A key whose matching `patternProperties` subschema names no type is stubbed that way too, where it used to be stubbed with that subschema itself and render as the unsupported field no type can draw. A key that matches none of an object's `patternProperties` patterns is stubbed from its data the same way, since a schema naming no `additionalProperties` allows that key as much as `additionalProperties: true` does; only a schema that forbids such a key still stubs it as `{ type: 'null' }`: an `additionalProperties: false`, an `unevaluatedProperties: false` where no `additionalProperties` evaluates the key, or a matching pattern of `false`, which rejects every value the key could hold. Which schema describes a key is `getAdditionalPropertySchema()`'s answer and the type it says the property holds is `getAdditionalPropertyType()`'s, both of which `ObjectField` reads to seed a new property, so the field a key renders with and the value it is seeded with cannot disagree. That subschema is resolved before the stub is built, through one ladder for every keyword that can describe a key: an `additionalProperties` carrying a `$ref` used to be resolved from that `$ref` alone, discarding the keywords beside it, where those keywords are now layered over the referenced ones as they are wherever else a `$ref` appears, and an `allOf` naming a type now names the stub's type instead of leaving it to the data. A subschema offering an `anyOf`/`oneOf` of options that disagree about the type is stubbed without a type of its own, where it used to be stubbed as an `object`, which rendered an empty object fieldset beside the option selector and seeded the `{}` no primitive option accepts; options that agree on a type, `object` included, still name it ([#5330](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5330)). When expanding every branch, `resolveAnyOrOneOfSchemas()` keeps the keyword it didn't read on each option it returns, so the schema parser also covers the `oneOf` of a schema whose `anyOf` it resolved first, which a precompiled validator otherwise threw for at runtime
- **`sanitizeDataForNewSchema()`**: A property is no longer treated as having changed schema when its type differs but both the old and the new type carry `GUESSED_TYPE_FLAG`, since those types describe the data rather than anything either schema requires. Without this, switching the type of such a property through the fallback UI discarded the value as data belonging to an old schema. A new schema that declares a type of its own is still the type the data has to satisfy. It also compares a `default` or `const` with the form data by value rather than by reference, so a `readOnly` object or array holding its default keeps it, and an untouched object default is swapped for the new schema's. An array item picked from object constants is kept when the new items schema still offers it, rather than sanitized property by property and dropped. An `anyOf`/`oneOf` with an option that is neither a `const` nor an `enum` no longer counts as a list of allowed values, so a value only that option accepts is kept rather than replaced with one of the constants. An option's `enum` counts every one of its values as allowed, where one with more than one value used to add none, so a value it allows is kept and one no option allows is cleared. Array items are no longer filtered against the items `enum` on every pass: with the previous form data passed (the way `Form` calls it), the filter is skipped when the old and new items schemas, each resolved against its own data, agree - so editing an unrelated field no longer silently drops values the schema offered when they were entered ([#5451](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5451)). Without previous data the filter always runs, matching v6. The filter always runs for a select over object or array constants and for a property the new schema introduces, and a scalar property outside the new `enum` follows the same agreement rule when previous data is available. Whether a schema or a property is an array is read through `getSchemaType()`, and two `type` lists are compared by what they list rather than as objects, so an array whose type list also allows `null`, such as `['array', 'null']`, keeps and sanitizes its items as a plain `type: 'array'` does, and a property whose type list two options each declare keeps its value across a switch. A tuple, an array declaring no `items` or the same boolean `items`, that both schemas describe alike is kept up to the new schema's `maxItems` rather than cleared, an array the new schema's `const` rejects is cleared so that the `const` can take its place, and a property or an array's items whose type list names `object`, such as `['object', 'null']`, is sanitized property by property when it holds an object, as a `type: 'object'` one is, keeping a `null` it allows ([#5359](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5359)). A value it sets, clears or sanitizes under a property named `__proto__` is written as an own key, where it was lost ([#5561](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5561))
- **`setByPath()`**: A `__proto__` segment, in any position, is written as an own key through [`setOwn()`](../api-reference/utility-functions.md#setown), where the path used to be refused and the write dropped. The write itself never reaches the prototype setter, but code of your own that builds the path from user input, such as a key the user typed or a dotted path from a URL, and then copies the result with `Object.assign()`, a `target[key] = source[key]` loop or a hand-written recursive merge does reach it, changing the copy's prototype or, through a recursive merge, `Object.prototype` itself. Copy such a result with spread or `setOwn()`, or leave `__proto__` out of the path. Under `additionalProperties: false`, AJV doesn't count a declared `__proto__` property among the declared ones, so a value written to it fails validation as an additional property ([#5561](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5561))
- **`getPropertySchema()`**: Reads `properties` by own key, so a property named like an `Object.prototype` member that the schema doesn't declare, such as `toString` or `constructor`, returns `{}` rather than the inherited function ([#5561](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5561))
- **`orderProperties()`**: Matches property names by own key. A property named `__proto__`, declared or held under `additionalProperties`, is placed at a `ui:order` wildcard, where it was left out, and a `ui:order` without a wildcard that doesn't list it now throws, where the property was silently hidden. `ObjectField` renders that throw as its `InvalidObjectField` error in place of the object, so add `__proto__` or `*` to such an order. A name like `toString` that the order lists but the properties don't is dropped rather than returned ([#5561](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5561))
- **`toErrorSchema()`** and **`ErrorSchemaBuilder`**: Write through `setByPath()`, so an error at a path through a `__proto__` key is filed under that key as an own key, where it was dropped. An error a `transformErrors` points at `.__proto__` reaches that field, since the validators build their `errorSchema` with `toErrorSchema()` after it runs ([#5561](https://github.com/rjsf-team/react-jsonschema-form/issues/5561))
